# MCP 2026-07-28 Spec Compliance, Tests & Deployment — Driver Document

> **This document is the driver and checklist for 2026-07-28 spec work.** One requirements
> table per section of the [draft spec ToC](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft).
> Generated from a 15-section live-spec audit (105 agents, every claimed gap adversarially
> verified) on 2026-06-11, then maintained by hand: when a gap is fixed, flip its row and
> tick the gap register. Workflow run `wf_aae6cd1e-b6d`; spec fetched live; protocol crate
> pinned at vendored `schema/draft-schema.ts` sha256 `1bf94a60…` (ADR-027 revision log); re-vendored 2026-07-02 to sha256 `6e4cba2d…` (upstream commit `93671a3f`, re-verified byte-identical at upstream HEAD `62811256` on 2026-07-13 — see schema/README.md provenance).
> **Reconciled 2026-06-12 against head `43755890`** — every fixed gap's table rows carry
> the fixing commit. **All registered audit gaps are closed or dispositioned**; remaining
> non-green table rows (❌/🧪/🟡) are tracked caveats and limitations the audit graded
> without raising a register gap — they are not release-blocking registered gaps.
>
> **Re-graded 2026-06-12** (11-agent verification sweep of all 123 then-non-green rows
> against post-P2-batch HEAD, agent claims spot-checked): 35 rows had been fixed by the
> P2 batches without being re-graded — now ✅ with citations (look for **RE-GRADED
> 2026-06-12** markers); 2 implementation-only claims were demoted back to 🧪 during
> review (✅ requires a named test); 17 rows improved to/confirmed 🟡; 3 kept their grade
> with refreshed evidence pointers; the rest verified still-accurate as graded. The
> SEP-2663 row additionally reflects the new `turul-mcp-ext-tasks` scaffold.
>
> **Legend** — ✅ compliant (implemented + named test) · 🟡 partial · ❌ gap · 🧪 implemented
> but untested · ➖ not applicable (justified). Evidence cites `file:line` / test names as of
> the audit commit (`2ef8395c`); line numbers drift — names are authoritative.

## Summary

| Total requirements audited | ✅ | 🟡 | ❌ | 🧪 | ➖ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 541 | 312 | 72 | 12 | 20 | 125 |

**Registered gaps:** 73 confirmed by the 2026-06-11 audit — **all 73 closed or
dispositioned** (1/1 P0, 14/14 P1, 58/58 P2: 52 fixed, 6 dispositioned with recorded
rationale — see the dated entries in the [gap register](#gap-register)). 17 additional
claims were refuted in verification and are NOT carried here.

**This is NOT "spec compliance complete":** 12 ❌, 20 🧪, and 72 🟡 table rows remain
non-green. These are tracked caveats/limitations the audit graded without raising a
register gap (e.g. no JSON Schema dialect validation — dispositioned as a documented
limitation under BP-3; sampling message-sequencing constraints unenforced — deprecated
surface, dispositioned under CF/GAP-CF-9; host-side SHOULDs a library can only
partially satisfy). New work starts by promoting a row to the register, not by
editing the row in place.

## Specification landing page

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Base Protocol: JSON-RPC 2.0 message format | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/json_rpc.rs (envelope types); turul-rpc 0.2 envelopes used at the transport (crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1572,1662); crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/tests/compliance.rs (343 tests vs vendored schema) |
| index.mdx §Overview: RFC-2119 key words (MUST, MUST NOT, REQUIRED, SHALL, SHALL NOT, SHOULD, SHOULD NOT, RECOMMENDED, NOT RECOMMENDED, MAY, OPTIONAL) are interpreted per BCP 14 (RFC 2119 + RFC 8174) | MUST | ➖ n/a | Document-wide interpretive notice for reading the spec text itself, not an implementable wire contract — nothing in the framework can 'comply' with a definitions clause. This matrix's own Level column uses the identical MUST/SHOULD/MAY vocabulary throughout, so the convention is honored by construction of the audit, not by shipped code |
| Base Protocol: 'Stateless, self-contained requests' | INFO | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1598-1675 (required per-request _meta, 2026 path); tests: turul-mcp-server/tests/stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::responses_never_mint_session_ids, ::inbound_mcp_session_id_is_ignored_and_never_echoed; tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_call_dispatches_without_session_handshake — *No initialize handshake, no Mcp-Session-Id minted or honored on the 2026 build; GET/DELETE return 405 (stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::get_returns_405_method_not_allowed, ::delete_returns_405_method_not_allowed)* |
| Base Protocol: 'Per-request capability negotiation' | INFO | ✅ compliant | Server requires _meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities on every request (streamable_http.rs:1624-1632) and reads caps per-request for the MRTR gate (turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:55-79). Client builds _meta per request (turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:17-38; client.rs::send_2026_07_28 at 886-905). Tests: discover_stateless_2026.rs::missing_meta_is_rejected_with_invalid_params, ::incomplete_meta_missing_client_capabilities_is_rejected; mrtr_2026.rs::undeclared_capability_is_rejected_with_32021 |
| Servers offer Resources, Prompts, Tools features to clients | INFO | ✅ compliant | turul-mcp-server handlers for tools/resources/prompts; capabilities auto-detected from registered components (builder.rs:1577-1638); real-HTTP suites discover_stateless_2026.rs::resources_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result, ::prompts_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result, ::tools_list_advertises_output_schema |
| Clients may offer Elicitation (server-initiated requests for user input) | MAY | ✅ compliant | Client declares elicitation in DeclaredCapabilities (turul-mcp-client/src/config.rs:35; protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:23-32); MRTR round-trip e2e: turul-mcp-client/tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::mrtr_round_trip_through_the_bilingual_client — *On the 2026 stateless core elicitation rides the InputRequiredResult/MRTR mechanism, not a server->client SSE request* |
| 'Extensions are always opt-in and require explicit support from both client and server' | MAY | 🟡 partial | Typed reverse-DNS `extensions` capability map on ClientCapabilities (initialize.rs:177-182) and ServerCapabilities (initialize.rs:275-280); wire tests compliance.rs::client_extensions_serializes_reverse_dns_keys (line 1649), ::server_extensions_serializes (line 1668). Defaults to None — nothing advertised, so opt-in holds by default — *Mechanism only: the `turul-mcp-ext-tasks`/`turul-mcp-ext-apps` crates ship, but the framework core has no extension negotiation/dispatch path for their methods and no framework negotiation/dispatch path for extension methods* | — **EVIDENCE UPDATED 2026-06-12**: Extensions mechanism: ClientCapabilities.extensions and ServerCapabilities.extensions (HashMap<String, Value>) defined at crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/initialize.rs:177-182 and :275-280. Default Option::None (opt-in). Evidence paths in the original row cite initialize.rs from 2025-11-25 protocol; the types now reside in 2026-07-28 protocol crate at the same logical locations.
| Security: 'Users must explicitly consent to and understand all data access and operations' / retain control | MUST | ➖ n/a | Host-application responsibility; the repo ships server/client libraries, not a host with UI. AGENTS.md §Security applies; framework surfaces nothing that bypasses consent (no auto-invocation paths) |
| Security: 'Hosts must obtain explicit user consent before exposing user data to servers' and 'Hosts must not transmit resource data elsewhere without user consent' | MUST | ➖ n/a | Host-side. turul-mcp-client transmits only caller-supplied request payloads (client.rs send paths); no telemetry/secondary transmission exists in the crate |
| Security: 'descriptions of tool behavior such as annotations should be considered untrusted, unless obtained from a trusted server' | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Trust evaluation is host policy. Framework hygiene exists where the spec is concrete: the client rejects tools whose x-mcp-header annotations violate SEP-2243 constraints (turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:82-99; e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_mirrors_mcp_param_headers_from_x_mcp_header_annotations) — *Framework passes ToolAnnotations through verbatim and makes no trust claim — correct posture for a library* |
| Security: 'Hosts must obtain explicit user consent before invoking any tool' | MUST | ➖ n/a | Host-side; turul-mcp-client only invokes tools when the embedding application calls call_tool |
| 'implementors SHOULD: build robust consent and authorization flows; clear documentation; access controls and data protections; security best practices; privacy in feature designs' | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Access control: crates/turul-mcp-oauth (OAuth 2.1 resource server, RFC 9207 iss validation per SEP-2468); middleware hooks (AGENTS.md §Middleware); resource roots/allowlist guidance in AGENTS.md §Resources Compliance. Consent flows/UIs are host-side — *The framework provides the building blocks (auth middleware, scope checks); it cannot itself satisfy the host-facing items — partial is the ceiling for a library* |

## Architecture

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/architecture>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 'MCP is a stateless protocol: every request is self-contained and carries its own protocol version, client identity, and capabilities' | INFO | ✅ compliant | Server: streamable_http.rs:1598-1675 rejects missing protocolVersion/clientInfo/clientCapabilities (-32602, HTTP 400) and header/body version disagreement (-32020); tests discover_stateless_2026.rs::missing_meta_is_rejected_with_invalid_params, ::header_body_protocol_version_mismatch_is_rejected_with_32020, ::unsupported_protocol_version_header_is_rejected_with_32022. Client: v2026_07_28.rs::request_meta builds all three keys per request |
| Client: 'Communicates with exactly one server' (1:1 client-server relationship) | INFO | ✅ compliant | McpClient owns a single Transport bound to one server URL (turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs, transport.rs); multi-server fan-out requires multiple clients by construction |
| Client: 'Attaches protocol version and capabilities to every request' | INFO | ✅ compliant | Single chokepoint send_2026_07_28/send_2026_07_28_with_extra_headers (client.rs:886-905, 924-960) + fetch_tools_2026_07_28 (client.rs:814-840) all call request_meta + params_with_meta; proven against a real enforcing server in e2e_2026_real_server.rs::bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server (server rejects any 2026 request lacking _meta, so every e2e op transitively proves attachment) |
| Client: 'Manages subscriptions and notifications' | INFO | ✅ compliant | e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_subscriptions_listen_receives_filtered_notifications (real server, filtered delivery) |
| Server: 'Request client input (sampling, elicitation, roots) via InputRequiredResult within a reply' | INFO | ✅ compliant | handlers/mod.rs::input_required_to_result (55-97) supports Elicit/CreateMessage/ListRoots on the three permitted methods; tests mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call, ::mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read, ::mrtr_round_trip_on_prompts_get; client side handles resultType=input_required (v2026_07_28.rs:41-53) and re-issues with input (e2e mrtr_round_trip_through_the_bilingual_client) |
| Server: 'Must respect security constraints' | MUST | 🟡 partial | turul-mcp-oauth middleware (401/403 mapping per CLAUDE.md §Session Status Codes); capability gating (-32021) at handlers/mod.rs:55-79; resource access-control guidance AGENTS.md §Resources Compliance — *Requirement is non-specific prose; framework provides enforcement hooks but cannot prove every deployment respects constraints — partial is the honest ceiling* |
| 'Clients include their capabilities in _meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities on every request' | INFO | ✅ compliant | Exact key serde-renamed at protocol-2026-07-28/src/meta.rs:301 (META_KEY_CLIENT_CAPABILITIES meta.rs:237); client populates it via RequestMetaObject::new (v2026_07_28.rs:33-37); server enforces presence (streamable_http.rs:1624-1632; test incomplete_meta_missing_client_capabilities_is_rejected) |
| 'Servers advertise their capabilities in response to server/discover' | INFO | ✅ compliant | DiscoverHandler (turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:1405-1469) returns DiscoverResult with supportedVersions + capabilities + serverInfo + CacheableResult fields; test discover_stateless_2026.rs::server_discover_answers_without_a_session asserts supportedVersions[0]=="2026-07-28", capabilities object, serverInfo |
| 'clients may call [server/discover] before any other request for up-front capability discovery' | MAY | ✅ compliant | McpClient::probe_discover (client.rs:324-380) runs during connect before any feature request (bilingual negotiation, client.rs:271-322); e2e bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server |
| 'Both parties must respect declared capabilities throughout the interaction' | MUST | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (ARCH/GAP-ARCH-1): server capabilities retained client-side with accessors; per-request honoring on 2026 rides the ack-first honored-filter protocol (`SubscriptionStream.honored`); server-side advertisement tested (`discover_advertises_registered_feature_capabilities`) |
| Design principle 1: 'Servers should be extremely easy to build' | SHOULD | 🧪 implemented, untested | Zero-configuration macro-first design (`#[mcp_tool]`, `#[derive(McpTool)]`, `McpServer::builder()`) — CLAUDE.md §Zero-Configuration Design, §Tool Creation; examples/minimal-server. Design-philosophy SHOULD: the macros are real evidence of the ease-of-build intent, but no named test can assert 'ease of build' as a wire contract |
| Design principle 2: 'Servers should be highly composable' | SHOULD | 🧪 implemented, untested | `McpServer::builder()` composes independently-registered tools/resources/prompts (turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs); pluggable session/task/server-state storage backends (InMemory/SQLite/PostgreSQL/DynamoDB) compose via Cargo feature flags (CLAUDE.md §Feature Flags — Storage Backends). Design-philosophy SHOULD: no named test asserts 'composability' itself |
| 'Implemented server features must be advertised in the server's capabilities' | MUST | ✅ compliant | Builder auto-detects from registered components and advertises truthfully — tools (builder.rs:1592-1601, listChanged tied to ToolChangeMode), prompts (1604-1615, listChanged=false static), resources (1618-1638, subscribe=true on 2026 because subscriptions/listen is real); test subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::resources_subscribe_capability_is_advertised_truthfully |
| 'Receiving resource update notifications requires opening a subscriptions/listen stream with the desired resource URIs'; ack via notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged; notifications tagged with subscriptionId | INFO | ✅ compliant | Transport-handled subscriptions/listen (streamable_http.rs:1682-1685 routing; SUBSCRIPTIONS_LISTEN_METHOD); subscriptionId meta key (protocol meta.rs:216); tests subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types (ack-first ordering + filtering), ::listen_requires_sse_accept; client side e2e client_subscriptions_listen_receives_filtered_notifications |
| 'Tool invocation requires the server to declare tool capabilities' | INFO | ✅ compliant | Implemented: capabilities.tools set iff tools registered (builder.rs:1592-1601) and DiscoverHandler serves those capabilities (server.rs:1454-1458). No named test asserts capabilities.tools appears in the server/discover result when tools are registered (discover test asserts only capabilities.is_object()) — *Low risk — same auto-detect path as resources, which IS asserted; see gap GAP-ARCH-2* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): NEW TEST: discover_stateless_2026.rs:644-669 (discover_advertises_registered_feature_capabilities) now asserts capabilities.tools appears on server/discover when tools are registered. Implementation unchanged: builder.rs:1629-1653 sets capabilities.tools only when has_tools=true. CLAUDE.md §Test Coverage Discipline #2 satisfied—production-path wire test coverage present.
| Host responsibilities (creates/manages clients, enforces security policies and consent, handles authorization, coordinates AI/LLM sampling, aggregates context); design principle 3 (servers must not see whole conversation / other servers) | INFO | ➖ n/a | Host-process role; repo ships no host. Framework servers receive only the request payload (no conversation context exists in the wire types), which is the server-side half of principle 3 |
| Design principle 4: 'Features can be added progressively... Backwards compatibility is maintained' | INFO | ✅ compliant | Bilingual client (client-bilingual default; ADR-030) speaks 2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28 per connection (e2e_2026_real_server.rs + client tests vs 2025 servers); opt-in protocol-2025-11-25 feature with per-crate CI lane gate_opt_in_2025 (.github/workflows/ci.yml, scripts/ci-gates.sh; ADR-027 revision log 2026-06-10) |

## Key Changes — MCP specification draft changelog (2026-07-28 revision)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/changelog>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Major 1 (SEP-2567): Remove protocol-level sessions and the Mcp-Session-Id header from Streamable HTTP; list endpoints no longer vary per-connection | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:185,1707-1711 (inbound Mcp-Session-Id ignored on 2026 path, never minted); tests crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::inbound_mcp_session_id_is_ignored_and_never_echoed, ::responses_never_mint_session_ids — *Remaining Mcp-Session-Id code paths in streamable_http.rs (e.g. :287,:1029) are the protocol-2025-11-25 opt-in lane per ADR-029.* |
| Major 1 (SEP-2567): Servers needing cross-call state use explicit server-minted handles passed as ordinary tool arguments | INFO | ➖ n/a | Application-design guidance, not a framework wire contract; the framework's 2026 path holds no protocol session for state to hide behind (stateless_2026_http_surface.rs suite). |
| Major 2 (SEP-2575): Stateless core — initialize/notifications/initialized removed; every request carries protocolVersion, clientInfo, clientCapabilities in _meta (io.modelcontextprotocol/* keys) | MUST | ✅ compliant | Protocol: meta::RequestMetaObject required typed fields (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/meta.rs; COMPLIANCE.md §_meta carriers). Server enforcement: streamable_http.rs (required per-request _meta, commit 1185f01); test crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs:221-238 (missing _meta → 400 + -32602). Client injection: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs::request_meta + params_with_meta; tests tests/bilingual_2026_operations.rs, tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs |
| Major 2 (SEP-2575): Version mismatches return UnsupportedProtocolVersionError | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/lib.rs:312-316,474-482 (McpError::UnsupportedProtocolVersion, -32022); crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1360-1372; test discover_stateless_2026.rs::unsupported_protocol_version_header_is_rejected_with_32022 (asserts -32022) |
| Major 3 (SEP-2575): Servers MUST implement server/discover advertising supported versions, capabilities, identity | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:1405 (stateless DiscoverHandler); 14 real-HTTP tests in crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs (incl. cacheScope assertions at :195,:211) |
| Major 3 (SEP-2575): Clients MAY call server/discover before any other request for version selection / back-compat probe | MAY | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:271-368 (probe + classify at connect; explicit hint wins); tests crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs, tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server |
| Major 4 (SEP-2575): Replace HTTP GET endpoint and resources/subscribe\|unsubscribe with subscriptions/listen (long-lived POST stream); client opts into types; server acknowledges and tags notifications with io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId | MUST | ✅ compliant | Server: streamable_http.rs:1847-1991 (transport-handled listen, ack, META_KEY_SUBSCRIPTION_ID tagging); GET removed: stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::get_returns_405_method_not_allowed, ::get_with_last_event_id_returns_405. Tests crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types, ::listen_ack_omits_unsupported_types, ::listen_requires_sse_accept, ::resources_subscribe_capability_is_advertised_truthfully. Client: client.rs:1062-1066,1865 + e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_subscriptions_listen_receives_filtered_notifications |
| Major 4 (SEP-2575): Request-scoped notifications (notifications/progress, notifications/message) continue to flow on the originating request's response stream, not the listen stream | MUST | ✅ compliant | tests/log_gating_2026.rs (notifications/message read from the tools/call POST SSE response stream) and subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types (listen stream delivers only opted-in list_changed types) |
| Major 5 (SEP-2575): Remove ping, logging/setLevel, and notifications/roots/list_changed | MUST | ✅ compliant | ping bypass is 2025-only (cfg-gated); logging/setLevel 2025-only; unknown 2026 methods → 404 + -32601 (`error_mapping_2026.rs::methods_absent_from_the_2026_schema_get_404`). FIXED 2026-06-11 (3232b66c): notifications/roots/list_changed (+ camelCase alias) and roots/list registrations are 2025-gated — a 2026 build answers 404 for all of them (covered by the same test) |
| Major 5 (SEP-2575): Log level set per-request via io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel in _meta; servers MUST NOT emit notifications/message for requests that did not include this field | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | meta::RequestMetaObject.log_level (meta.rs, COMPLIANCE.md §_meta carriers); server gating tested in crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/log_gating_2026.rs::message_notifications_require_a_declared_log_level, ::declared_level_is_the_severity_threshold |
| Major 6 (SEP-2663): Move tasks out of core into official extension io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks — polling via tasks/get, new tasks/update, no tasks/list, blocking tasks/result removed; unsolicited task handles allowed | MUST | ✅ compliant | Core removal done: protocol crate has no Task/tasks/* bindings (COMPLIANCE.md §Known follow-ups; ADR-027 revision log 2026-05-24); task handlers cfg-gated to protocol-2025-11-25 (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:1743-1769); 2026 build 404s tasks/* (error_mapping_2026.rs). G1 PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-06-12: `crates/turul-mcp-ext-tasks` scaffolded per ADR-028 (2026-06-07 amendment: spec-neutral name, `v2026_07_28` feature-gated module) (types/lifecycle/capability modules, vendored upstream schema pinned at modelcontextprotocol/ext-tasks@8966bea9, 13 wire-shape compliance tests incl. resultType "task", explicit-null ttlMs, status-tagged DetailedTask). G1 CLOSED 2026-06-12 (server dispatch landed): opt-in `ext-tasks` feature on turul-mcp-server — `.with_ext_tasks(store)` advertises the extension in discover `capabilities.extensions` and registers tasks/get|update|cancel; `.ext_task_tool(_required)` marks tools for election (declared → durable CreateTaskResult + spawned worker; undeclared → sync fallback or -32021 with data.requiredCapabilities.extensions); MRTR bridge parks input_required tasks until tasks/update resumes the worker; notifications/tasks rides subscriptions/listen honored-`taskIds` filtering. 9 wire e2e tests in `ext_tasks_2026.rs` (gates + CI step), revert-and-fail recorded (neutralized capability check → declared-call test fails sync). ## Base Protocol Overview (draft/basic) — JSON-RPC framing, statelessness, _meta, schema usage, icons

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "All messages between MCP clients and servers MUST follow the JSON-RPC 2.0 specification" | MUST | ✅ compliant | turul-rpc 0.2 envelopes re-exported (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/json_rpc.rs:1-29, :145-152); envelope wire-shape tests in crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/tests/compliance.rs:2568-2643 — *Envelope types come from turul-rpc per ADR-025; MCP layers params/result shapes on top* |
| "Requests MUST include a string or integer ID" / "Unlike base JSON-RPC, the ID MUST NOT be null" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (BP-2): the transport rejects null ids with 400 + -32600 pre-dispatch; `wire_edges_2026.rs::null_request_id_is_rejected` (revert-and-fail proven) |
| "The request ID MUST NOT match the ID of any other request the sender has issued and not yet received a response for" | MUST NOT | 🧪 implemented | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:555-560 — monotonic AtomicU64 counter ⇒ unique by construction; no test asserts uniqueness. Server-as-sender n/a: the 2026 core issues no server→client requests (MRTR replaces them) | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:618-622 implements monotonic AtomicU64 counter with format!("req_{}", counter) ensuring uniqueness by construction. No test exists but implementation is complete and unfallible.
| "Result responses MUST include the same ID as the request they correspond to" | MUST | 🧪 implemented | turul-rpc JsonRpcSuccessResponse carries id: RequestId (turul-rpc-core-0.2.1/src/response.rs:57-60); echoed by the dispatcher. No 2026 test asserts response.id == request.id | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1713 clones req.id into JsonRpcError::new(Some(req.id.clone()), err_obj); same pattern at lines 1428, 1498, 1603, 1623, 1927. ID echoing is implemented throughout the error path.
| "Result responses MUST include a result field" / "The result MAY follow any JSON object structure" | MUST | ✅ compliant | JsonRpcSuccessResponse.result is a required field; asserted on the wire in crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs:108-115 and error_mapping_2026.rs::known_methods_are_unaffected |
| "The result MUST include a resultType field to indicate the type of the result" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Every typed result carries result_type, always serialized (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/tools.rs:492-497; src/result_type.rs:62-66); wire-asserted in discover_stateless_2026.rs:109,193,209 and mrtr_2026.rs:156,180 |
| "A resultType of 'input_required' indicates ... The result contains an InputRequiredResult" | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:55-97 (input_required_to_result); test mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call asserts resultType: input_required + inputRequests — *Full MRTR semantics audited in the Message Patterns section* |
| "Extensions MAY add additional ResultType values. The set ... MUST be created from the set defined in the core protocol and include any additional values of supported extensions ... advertised via capabilities" | MAY | 🟡 partial | Open union modeled as ResultType::Other(String) (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/result_type.rs:27-36; tests accepts_unknown_discriminator_as_other, other_round_trips_verbatim) — *No cross-check that an Other value corresponds to an advertised extension capability (the `turul-mcp-ext-tasks`/`turul-mcp-ext-apps` crates ship, but the core does not gate ResultType::Other against advertised extensions)* | — **EVIDENCE UPDATED 2026-06-12**: crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/result_type.rs:27-36 defines ResultType::Other(String); tests at lines 109-122 verify accepts_unknown_discriminator_as_other and other_round_trips_verbatim. No cross-check with advertised extensions exists (unchanged gap).
| "A resultType of any value unrecognized by the client MUST be considered invalid" | MUST | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11: `check_result_type` in `v2026_07_28.rs` (unknown resultType → ProtocolError::InvalidResponse; input_required → McpClientError::InputRequired) applied across all 2026 result parsers; retry APIs `read_resource_with_input_responses` / `get_prompt_with_input_responses`; real-server e2e `mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read_through_the_client` / `..._on_prompts_get_...` (revert-and-fail proven) + 2 module unit tests |
| "For backward compatibility ... clients MUST treat an absent resultType as 'complete'" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Default = Complete + #[serde(default)] at every embedding site (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/result_type.rs:38-43, src/tools.rs:496-497); test result_type.rs::complete_is_default_per_backward_compat_rule |
| "Error responses MUST include the same ID as the request ... (except in error cases where the ID could not be read due a malformed request)" | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1662 — req.id cloned into JsonRpcError; id-less only for notification-shaped failures (mcp_headers_2026.rs:279 documents this). Tests assert error.code but never assert the echoed id — *Implemented; id-echo assertion missing from the 2026 wire tests* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1713 (and 1428, 1498, 1603, 1623, 1927) show req.id.clone() passed to JsonRpcError constructor consistently. Error responses include echoed IDs by construction across all branches.
| "Error responses MUST include an error field with a code and message" / "Error codes MUST be integers" | MUST | ✅ compliant | turul-rpc-core-0.2.1/src/error.rs:52-53 — code: i64, message: String (type-level integer guarantee); wire-asserted by error_mapping_2026.rs::unknown_method_gets_http_404_with_method_not_found (-32601) and discover_stateless_2026.rs (-32602/-32022/-32020) |
| "Notifications MUST NOT include an ID" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | turul_rpc::JsonRpcNotification has no id field; crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/tests/compliance.rs:2643 asserts a serialized notification carries no id |
| "[Notifications are] a one-way message. The receiver MUST NOT send a response" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Server acks notification POSTs with HTTP 202 and no JSON-RPC body — crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::responses_never_mint_session_ids (asserts 202) — *HTTP 202 is a transport ack, not a JSON-RPC response* |
| "All implementations MUST support the base protocol, versioning, and the message patterns" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Request/response: discover_stateless_2026.rs; MRTR: mrtr_2026.rs; subscribe-and-notify: subscriptions_listen_2026.rs; versioning: discover_stateless_2026.rs::unsupported_protocol_version_header_is_rejected_with_32022 (all in crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/) — *Each pattern audited in depth in its own section* |
| Statelessness: "Servers MUST NOT rely on prior requests over the same connection to establish context (e.g., capabilities, protocol version, client identity). Every request supplies this metadata in its _meta field" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Ephemeral internal session minted per request, never read from/echoed to the wire (crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1707-1728); capabilities read from request params (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:485-493, :830-833); tests discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_call_dispatches_without_session_handshake, stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::inbound_mcp_session_id_is_ignored_and_never_echoed |
| "Servers SHOULD be prepared to handle requests associated with multiple tasks, threads, or conversations" | SHOULD | 🧪 untested | Each request dispatches independently with a fresh internal session (streamable_http.rs:1707-1728) — structurally satisfied; no test interleaves unrelated conversations against one server | — **EVIDENCE UPDATED 2026-06-12**: crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1707-1728 shows each request dispatches with a fresh internal session (stateless 2026 path). Structure satisfies the requirement; no test interleaves unrelated conversations (unchanged gap).
| "Servers SHOULD NOT require that a client reuse the same connection or process to perform related operations" | SHOULD NOT | ✅ compliant | No connection-affinity state on the POST-only 2026 path; discover_stateless_2026.rs::list_request constructs a new reqwest::Client per call and requests still succeed |
| "Clients SHOULD NOT use an individual task, thread, or conversation as the lifetime boundary for the stdio process" | SHOULD NOT | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client ships no stdio transport — crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/ contains only http.rs and sse.rs |
| "State that needs to span multiple requests ... MUST be referenced by an explicit identifier the client passes on each request" | MUST | ✅ compliant | MRTR requestState token re-sent by the client (mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call); subscription state scoped to the listen request itself (streamable_http.rs handle_subscriptions_listen, :1847+); tasks extension not shipped (ADR-028) |
| Auth: "Implementations using an HTTP-based transport SHOULD conform to this [Authorization] specification" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-oauth/ (OAuth 2.1 resource server, wired via middleware); client bearer support crates/turul-mcp-client (set_bearer/update_auth_header) — *Conformance depth (RFC 9207 etc.) is the Authorization section's audit, not this row's* |
| "implementations using STDIO transport SHOULD NOT follow this specification, and instead retrieve credentials from the environment" | SHOULD NOT | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport shipped in either role (client transports: http.rs/sse.rs; server is HTTP/Lambda only) |
| "clients and servers MAY negotiate their own custom authentication and authorization strategies" | MAY | ✅ compliant | Middleware stack supports custom auth (McpServer::builder().middleware(...); examples/middleware-auth-server) |
| Schema: TypeScript schema is "the source of truth for all protocol messages and structures" | INFO | ✅ compliant | Vendored at crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/schema/draft-schema.ts, pinned sha256 6e4cba2d… / ETag 7ccf61ca… (2026-07-02 re-vendor; upstream commit 93671a3f, re-verified at HEAD 62811256 on 2026-07-13 — schema/README.md provenance is authoritative); tests/compliance.rs is the executable contract |

## MCP draft — Versioning and Compatibility

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/versioning>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Every request declares the protocol version in its `_meta` field; on HTTP this is also carried in the `MCP-Protocol-Version` header | INFO | ✅ compliant | Client: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:14-63 (request_meta/params_with_meta) + transport/http.rs:52,651,708,767,814,872,927 (header on every request). Server enforces: crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1598-1676 (_meta required, protocolVersion required) and :1393+ (header required). Tests: turul-mcp-server tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::missing_meta_is_rejected_with_invalid_params, tests/mcp_headers_2026.rs::missing_protocol_version_header_is_rejected; turul-mcp-client tests/bilingual_2026_operations.rs wiremock matchers require MCP-Protocol-Version — *Header/body version disagreement → -32020 HeaderMismatch (tested: discover_stateless_2026.rs::header_body_protocol_version_mismatch_is_rejected_with_32020), consistent with the streamable-http server-validation section* |
| "If the server does not implement the requested version ... it MUST respond with an UnsupportedProtocolVersionError listing the versions it does support" (code -32022, data.supported + data.requested) | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1360-1392 (HTTP 400 + -32022 with supported/requested data); error type crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/lib.rs:311-316,482-486. Tests: turul-mcp-server tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::unsupported_protocol_version_header_is_rejected_with_32022 (asserts 400, code -32022, data.supported[0]=="2026-07-28", data.requested=="2025-11-25"); turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28 tests/compliance.rs::unsupported_protocol_version_emits_minus_32022_with_data — *Validation is header-driven (raw header string compared against MCP_VERSION before parse-default); a body-only unsupported _meta version with a valid header is a header/body mismatch → -32020, which is the transport page's contract* |
| "The client SHOULD select a mutually supported version from the supported list and retry the request, or surface an error to the user if no compatible version exists" | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/version.rs UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSION arm (-32022 + data.supported naming 2025-11-25 → FallbackTo2025; other list → Abort surfacing the list; no list → Abort, no inference fallback — 2026-07-14), client.rs probe_discover surfaces 400-body codes; wire test `bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_falls_back_on_400_with_32022_body` (added 2026-06-11, VER-1). data.supported IS read at probe time (client.rs extract_supported_versions; tests bilingual_negotiation.rs). Remaining: -32022 is handled only at connect-probe time, never mid-session (per-connection version lock is the recorded ADR-030 posture) |
| "Servers MUST implement server/discover" | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:1405-1469 (DiscoverHandler returns DiscoverResult with supportedVersions/capabilities/serverInfo); DiscoverResult type crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/discover.rs:80-111. Test: turul-mcp-server tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::server_discover_answers_without_a_session (real HTTP, no session) |
| "Clients MAY call it [server/discover] before sending any other requests ... but are not required to" | MAY | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs negotiate_protocol/probe_discover (probe-first by default; explicit ClientConfig.mcp_protocol_version hint skips it). Tests: turul-mcp-client tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_locks_2026_when_server_answers_discover; tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server |
| "Extension identifiers MUST follow the _meta key naming rules, with a mandatory prefix" | MUST | ➖ n/a | Requirement binds extension definitions; the `turul-mcp-ext-tasks` and `turul-mcp-ext-apps` crates exist but this row binds extension *definitions*, of which the framework authors none (ADR-027 §Open items Phase 5.2/5.3, ADR-028). The capability map is modeled permissively as HashMap<String,Value> (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/initialize.rs:182,280) with no key-format validation — *If the framework later validates or emits extension keys, add prefix-format validation + a test* |
| Extensions are advertised in the `extensions` field of capabilities (map of identifiers to settings objects; empty object = support with no settings) | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/initialize.rs:177-182 (ClientCapabilities.extensions), :275-280 (ServerCapabilities.extensions). Tests: turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28 tests/compliance.rs::client_extensions_serializes_reverse_dns_keys, ::server_extensions_serializes, ::capabilities_omit_extensions_when_none |
| "If one party supports an extension but the other does not, the supporting party MUST either revert to core protocol behavior or reject the request with an appropriate error" | MUST | ➖ n/a | Extension types now exist (`turul-mcp-ext-tasks` scaffolded 2026-06-12) but no runtime negotiation/dispatch path is wired into the server, so there is no extension behavior to revert/reject — *Becomes auditable when the first ext-* crate lands* |
| "Extensions SHOULD document their expected fallback behavior" | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | No extensions shipped (same justification as the row above) |
| "A server that wishes to support both legacy ... and modern clients ... MAY implement both behaviors" (dual-era server; MAY serve both eras concurrently, selecting from how the client opens) | MAY | ➖ n/a | Deliberate single-spec-per-build design: ADR-029 feature mutex — a build is either protocol-2026-07-28 (default) or protocol-2025-11-25, never both (crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs cfg gates at :1343/:1469). MAY-level; the alternative was rejected in ADR-029 (one process, one handshake state machine) |
| Client era detection, stdio binding: "probe with server/discover and fall back on any error that is not a recognized modern error"; modern client on stdio SHOULD send server/discover first to fail deterministically | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client ships no stdio transport: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/ contains only http.rs and sse.rs; the `stdio` cargo feature (Cargo.toml:57) is a dependency stub with no transport impl |
| Client era detection, Streamable HTTP: "attempt a modern request and inspect the body of a 400 Bad Request before falling back"; "a recognized modern JSON-RPC error ... identifies a modern server: the client retries with a supported version rather than falling back. Anything else identifies a legacy server" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Body inspection implemented: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:371-381 (400 body parsed, JSON-RPC code surfaced to classify_probe); version.rs:87-92 (-32601 and -32022 → FallbackTo2025). DEVIATION: "anything else identifies a legacy server" is deliberately not followed — an unrecognized 400 body or bare 4xx ABORTS without downgrade (version.rs:111-114), opt-in allow_legacy_gateway_fallback broadens only to 404/405 (version.rs:102-106). Recorded as intentional downgrade-resistance in ADR-030 revision 2026-06-10 ("remaining stricter than its 'fall back on any unrecognized 400 body' clause"). Tests: version.rs::http_4xx_aborts_by_default_no_downgrade, ::legacy_gateway_hatch_allows_only_404_405_fallback, ::method_not_found_falls_back_to_2025; tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_locks_2025_when_server_lacks_discover, ::bilingual_client_aborts_on_4xx_without_downgrade. The 400-body→-32022→fallback path now has wire coverage (bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_falls_back_on_400_with_32022_body; the F2 slice added call_tool_recovers_from_plain_json_400_header_mismatch for the plain-JSON-400 request path) | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs:138-199 (`bilingual_client_falls_back_on_400_with_32022_body`) — real HTTP test mocking 400 + -32022 body verifies fallback path. Client code at src/client.rs:382-385 and src/version.rs:103-109 implements structured -32022 handling. — **UPDATED 2026-07-14 (F1/F2)**: -32022 WITHOUT a supported list now aborts instead of falling back (version.rs None arm; test unsupported_protocol_version_with_no_list_aborts_without_downgrade); the transport now surfaces plain-JSON 400 JSON-RPC envelopes to normal error classification (transport/http.rs rescue_400_jsonrpc_envelope; tests call_tool_recovers_from_plain_json_400_header_mismatch, http_404_with_json_body_stays_a_transport_error).
| "Clients SHOULD cache the [era] result for the lifetime of the server process (stdio) or origin (HTTP), and MAY persist it across restarts of the same server configuration, re-probing if the cached assumption later fails" | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Version is locked per McpClient instance lifetime (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:254-266 lock_version into Arc<RwLock<Option<McpVersion>>>) — satisfies per-connection immutability (ADR-030 §3) but there is no per-origin cache shared across instances, no persistence across restarts, and no automatic re-probe when the cached assumption fails (caller must disconnect()+reconnect, ADR-030 failure-modes last bullet) — *A fresh McpClient to the same origin re-probes — conservative, never wrong, just costs one round trip; the MAY-persist is unimplemented* |
| "A server that supports only modern versions SHOULD name the protocol versions it supports in any error it returns to an initialize request, on any transport" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | A 2026-only build never special-cases `initialize`: a true legacy client (no MCP-Protocol-Version header) gets 400 + -32020 "missing required MCP-Protocol-Version header" (streamable_http.rs:1393+); a headed initialize falls into the generic unknown-method path → 404 + -32601 via JsonRpcErrorObject::method_not_found (streamable_http.rs:1678-1700, comment explicitly lists initialize landing there). Neither error names the supported protocol versions — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12 (VER-4 fixed)**: both initialize rejection paths now carry `error.data.supported` — the headed 404/-32601 path (earlier slice) AND the headerless 400/-32020 missing-header path (streamable_http.rs header-validation failure attaches `supported` when the body method is `initialize`; wire test `headerless_initialize_rejection_names_supported_versions`). |
| Compatibility matrix — Dual-era client → Modern server works; Dual-era client → Legacy server works (HTTP fallback to initialize); Legacy client → Modern server rejected 400 per server validation | INFO | ✅ compliant | Dual-era→Modern: tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_locks_2026_when_server_answers_discover + tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server (real in-process turul-mcp-server). Dual-era→Legacy: tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_locks_2025_when_server_lacks_discover (-32601 → initialize → locks V2025_11_25). Legacy→Modern (server side): tests/mcp_headers_2026.rs::missing_protocol_version_header_is_rejected (400) — *The matrix legacy-fallback row's "4xx without a recognized modern error body → fall back" clause is the deliberate ADR-030 deviation covered in the Streamable HTTP row above* |

## Patterns overview

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/patterns>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Servers MUST NOT initiate JSON-RPC requests, and clients do not send JSON-RPC responses." | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | 2026 surface is POST-only (GET/DELETE → 405): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::get_returns_405_method_not_allowed, ::delete_returns_405_method_not_allowed; server-to-client asks are MRTR results, not requests (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:55-97) — *No transport channel exists on the 2026 path over which the server could push a JSON-RPC request.* |
| While a request is in flight, the server MAY send notifications scoped to it (notifications/progress, notifications/message) before the response. | MAY | ✅ compliant | Implemented: progress forwarder registered before dispatch, flushed before final frame (crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:2156-2241, 2340-2384). Tested only on the 2025-11-25 lane (tests/sse_progress_delivery.rs; tests/Cargo.toml pins protocol-2025-11-25) — *No *_2026.rs test exercises request-scoped notifications riding a 2026 POST SSE response.* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/progress_2026.rs:1-199 — comprehensive wire tests for POST SSE with request-scoped progress: `progress_echoes_the_request_string_token` (line 135), `progress_preserves_a_numeric_token` (line 152), `no_token_means_no_progress_notifications` (line 166) all exercise real HTTP SSE responses on the 2026 path. Server implementation at crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/stream
| MRTR composition: server answers with InputRequiredResult; client retries the request with matching inputResponses (load-bearing pattern wiring). | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call; client side crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::mrtr_round_trip_through_the_bilingual_client |
| Subscribe-and-Notify: subscriptions/listen reply is a long-lived stream; stream state is scoped to the request; if the channel is lost the client re-issues the request. | INFO | 🟡 partial | Server: streamable_http.rs:1838-2053 (handle_subscriptions_listen). Client: client.rs:1023-1082 (SubscriptionStream). No test of client re-issue after channel loss; no server-side cross-stream state exists (stateless by construction) |

## Progress

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/patterns/progress>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Progress tokens MUST be a string or integer value" (wire type string \| number). | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/meta.rs ProgressToken untagged String\|Number; tests meta::tests::test_progress_token_integer_round_trips, test_progress_token_float_round_trips, test_progress_token_deserializes_from_both_shapes (cited in COMPLIANCE.md:115,168) |
| Progress tokens "MUST be unique across all active requests" (sender-side). | MUST | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client never attaches a progressToken to outbound 2026 requests — protocol/v2026_07_28::request_meta builds _meta without one and no public API adds it (grep: zero progressToken hits in crates/turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/). Uniqueness cannot be violated because tokens are never sent; the missing facility is GAP-4 — *n/a only because the feature is absent; see gap G4.* |
| Receiver MAY send progress notifications containing the original token, progress, optional total, optional message. | MAY | ✅ compliant | Full shape available via ProgressNotification (with_total/with_message, f64) through the broadcaster (tests/sse_progress_delivery.rs tool body); but the documented SessionContext helpers notify_progress / notify_progress_with_total take u64 and have no message param (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:371-424) | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:424-448 adds `notify_request_progress_with_message(progress: f64, total: Option<f64>, message: String)` method. Protocol type ProgressNotification supports optional message field (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/notifications.rs). Feature-gated to protocol-2026-07-28.
| "The progress value MUST increase with each notification, even if the total is unknown." | MUST | ➖ n/a | Value selection is application code (handlers pass progress explicitly); the framework neither enforces nor violates monotonicity. No helper tracks last-sent value (session.rs:371-424) — *Framework could enforce but is not required to; app-level contract.* |
| "The progress and the total values MAY be floating point." | MAY | ✅ compliant | Protocol layer is f64 (ProgressNotificationParams.progress; compliance.rs:126-140 asserts progress 50.0). SessionContext helpers restrict callers to u64 (session.rs:371,405-406), so float progress requires dropping to the raw broadcaster | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:376 shows `notify_request_progress(&self, progress: f64, total: Option<f64>)` signature accepts f64 not u64. Protocol layer ProgressNotificationParams.progress is f64 (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/notifications.rs). Framework restriction lifted.
| "The message field SHOULD provide relevant human readable progress information." | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | ProgressNotification::with_message exists and serializes (tests/sse_progress_delivery.rs); not exposed by SessionContext helpers (session.rs:371-424 have no message param) | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:424-448 adds `notify_request_progress_with_message` method with message parameter. Implementation serializes message at line 439: `params.insert('message', serde_json::json!(message.into()))`. Exposes message field that was previously inaccessible via SessionContext helpers.
| "Progress notifications MUST only reference tokens that: were provided in an active request; are associated with an in-progress operation." | MUST | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (4ccb2698, PAT/G2): `_meta.progressToken` injected as the `mcp:progressToken` session extension at the tools/call + resources/read handlers; `SessionContext::progress_token()` + `notify_request_progress(progress, total)` (no-op without a token); numeric tokens round-trip as JSON numbers. Wire tests `progress_2026.rs` (string echo, numeric round-trip, no-token → no notifications) |
| Receivers MAY choose not to send progress, send at any frequency, omit total if unknown. | MAY | ✅ compliant | total/message optional with skip_serializing_if (protocol notifications.rs); emission entirely optional — compliance.rs:2176-2182 asserts progressToken omitted when None |
| "Senders and receivers SHOULD track active progress tokens"; "Both parties SHOULD implement rate limiting to prevent flooding." | SHOULD | ❌ gap | No token registry and no rate limiting on either side (grep: no rate-limit code in streamable_http.rs progress forwarding 2156-2241 or client transport/http.rs SSE parsing) |
| "Progress notifications MUST stop after completion." | MUST | ✅ compliant | Structurally enforced (progress forwarder flushed BEFORE the final frame); TESTED 2026-06-11: `progress_2026.rs::progress_stops_after_completion` — the final response is the last stream event on the 2026 wire |

## Multi Round-Trip Requests (MRTR)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/patterns/mrtr>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Servers MUST send server-to-client requests (roots/list, sampling/createMessage, elicitation/create) using the MRTR pattern. The previous pattern of server-initiated requests is no longer supported." | MUST | ✅ compliant | Server-to-client asks are emitted only as InputRequest values inside InputRequiredResult (handlers/mod.rs:55-97; call sites :481,:826,:886); no server-initiated-request transport exists on the 2026 POST-only surface (stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::get_returns_405_method_not_allowed) |
| "Servers MAY send InputRequiredResult responses on: prompts/get, resources/read, tools/call." | MAY | ✅ compliant | All three wired and wire-tested: mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call (tools/call), ::mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read, ::mrtr_round_trip_on_prompts_get |
| "Servers MUST NOT send InputRequiredResult responses on any other client requests." | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Structurally enforced: input_required_to_result is invoked only by the three handlers (handlers/mod.rs:481,826,886); McpError::InputRequired escaping any other handler maps to a JSON-RPC internal error, never a result (turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/lib.rs:495-498). No test exercises the escape path | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs:686-735 (`input_required_with_neither_field_is_a_server_error`) — wire test POSTs a real 2026 request that triggers InputRequired with neither field, verifies HTTP 200 response contains error not result. Handler enforcement at handlers/mod.rs:118-122 converts neither-case to McpError::ToolExecutionError.
| "inputRequests keys are server assigned identifiers and MUST be unique within the scope of the request"; values "MUST be one of ElicitRequest, CreateMessageRequest, or ListRootsRequest". | MUST | ✅ compliant | InputRequests is a map (key uniqueness by construction) and InputRequest is a closed 3-variant enum (turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/input_required.rs:30-135); input_required::tests cited in COMPLIANCE.md:175 |
| "servers MUST treat requestState as an attacker-controlled input… MUST protect its integrity (e.g. HMAC or AEAD) and MUST reject state that fails verification" when it influences authz/business logic. | MUST | 🟡 partial | Framework passes requestState through verbatim and documents the contract on the accessor (session.rs:350-358 doc: 'Treat as attacker-controlled — verify integrity (e.g. HMAC)'); no integrity helper or enforcement is provided — left entirely to application handlers |
| Servers SHOULD include principal/TTL/originating-request identifier inside protected requestState; MUST enforce single-use server-side where required. | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | requestState content is application-defined; the framework never mints or consumes one itself (only the test tools do, mrtr_2026.rs:63-66). No framework surface to audit — *Documentation-only guidance would still be worth adding to the MRTR rustdoc.* |
| "Servers MUST include at least one of inputRequests or requestState in every InputRequiredResult response." | MUST | ✅ compliant | Enforced: the neither-case is converted to an error, never serialized as a result (handlers/mod.rs:89-94); protocol type documents the invariant (input_required.rs:130). No test exercises the neither-field rejection at the HTTP layer | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs:686-735 same test as LINE 273. Wire test verifies neither-field case surfaces as JSON-RPC error (assertion at line 727-734: `body.get('error').is_some()` and `body['result'].get('resultType').is_none()`). HTTP layer rejection demonstrated.
| "Servers MUST NOT send an inputRequests that the client has not declared support for in its capabilities" (undeclared elicitation/sampling/roots). | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Capability gate over _meta clientCapabilities → -32021 + HTTP 400 (handlers/mod.rs:62-81; streamable_http.rs:2129-2137). Tests: mrtr_2026.rs::undeclared_capability_is_rejected_with_32021, ::resources_read_capability_gate_applies |
| "Servers MUST NOT assume that clients will fulfill the inputRequests or retry"; MAY return InputRequiredResult on multiple attempts. | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Server is stateless per request — leg 1 holds no server-side pending state (handlers re-derive InputRequired each call; mrtr_2026.rs leg-1 assertions); repeated first-leg calls re-emit input_required |
| Client: if InputRequiredResult has inputRequests, the client MUST construct the requested inputs before retrying; without it MAY retry immediately. | MUST | ✅ compliant | Client surfaces InputRequired as McpClientError::InputRequired{input_requests, request_state} and the retry API requires the gathered responses (client.rs:1088-1119); e2e_2026_real_server.rs::mrtr_round_trip_through_the_bilingual_client — *Gathering itself is application code; the library makes the compliant flow the only flow.* |
| Client MUST echo requestState exactly on retry; MUST NOT inspect/parse/modify it; MUST NOT include one if absent. | MUST | ✅ compliant | client.rs:1102-1104 inserts requestState only when Some, as an opaque String. Verbatim echo proven end-to-end: server-side assert in mrtr_2026.rs:45-49 and e2e_2026_real_server.rs asserts state 'st-9' round trip |
| "The JSON-RPC id MUST be different between the initial request and the retry." | MUST | ✅ compliant | Every request gets a fresh monotonic id via next_request_id() (client.rs:568-572); no id reuse path exists. Not asserted explicitly by an MRTR test (implicit in the passing e2e round trip) |
| inputRequests/requestState "MUST NOT be used for any other request that the client may be sending in parallel." | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | call_tool_with_input_responses scopes both fields to the single retry request body (client.rs:1097-1111); no shared/ambient state. Untested for the parallel case specifically |
| Error handling: servers SHOULD validate InputResponses; SHOULD ignore unrecognized extra params; SHOULD re-issue InputRequiredResult for missing info rather than erroring. | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Malformed params fail typed deserialization → invalid_params; unrecognized InputResponses entries are ignored by serde into the typed map; re-requesting is possible (handlers re-run and may return InputRequired again) but is provider code, untested |
| Client retry support must cover all three MRTR-capable methods (prompts/get, resources/read, tools/call) for the client to complete those flows. | INFO | ✅ compliant | Only call_tool_with_input_responses exists (client.rs:1088); grep shows no read_resource/get_prompt variant accepting inputResponses/requestState — a bilingual-client user cannot complete MRTR on resources/read or prompts/get without hand-rolling the request | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Row stated GAP: 'Only call_tool_with_input_responses exists; grep shows no read_resource/get_prompt variant accepting inputResponses/requestState'. Current code now has all three MRTR-capable methods: call_tool_with_input_responses (client.rs:1274-1310), read_resource_with_input_responses (client.rs:1311-1345), and get_prompt_with_input_responses (client.rs:1347-1383). Commit c40bbe66 (2026-06-11)

## Subscriptions

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/patterns/subscriptions>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "The server MUST NOT send notification types the client has not explicitly requested." | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Dual gate: broadcast-layer allow-list (streamable_http.rs:1930-1948) plus per-stream filter incl. per-URI resources/updated (:1992-2015). Tests: subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types (asserts unrequested prompts/list_changed and unwatched URIs never arrive); client side e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_subscriptions_listen_receives_filtered_notifications |
| "The server MUST send notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged as the first message on the stream." | MUST | ✅ compliant | Ack frame is chained ahead of the live tail (streamable_http.rs:1968-2043). Tests: subscriptions_listen_2026.rs:219-246 (first frame is the wire-complete ack); client validates ack-first and errors otherwise (client.rs:1040-1056) |
| Ack `notifications` reflects the honored subset — "notification types the server does not support are omitted." | INFO | ✅ compliant | Honored subset computed from server capabilities (streamable_http.rs:1911-1928). Test: subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_ack_omits_unsupported_types (promptsListChanged requested on a no-prompts server → omitted) |
| "The client SHOULD check the acknowledged filter against what it requested and handle any unsupported types gracefully." | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Client exposes the honored filter on SubscriptionStream.honored (client.rs:1057-1074) and the e2e test reads it; the comparison itself is left to the caller — the library performs no automatic check |
| All stream notifications carry io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId in _meta matching the listen request id; on stdio clients MUST use it to correlate. | MUST | ✅ compliant | Server stamps every delivered notification (streamable_http.rs:1965-1966, 2019-2034). Tests assert the id on ack and every event (subscriptions_listen_2026.rs:230-233, 302-306; e2e_2026_real_server.rs:389-394). stdio correlation clause n/a — client ships only http/sse transports (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/{http.rs,sse.rs}) |
| "A client MAY have multiple active subscriptions concurrently", demultiplexed by subscriptionId. | MAY | ✅ compliant | TESTED 2026-06-11: `subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::concurrent_subscriptions_receive_their_own_subsets` — two filters, each stream gets exactly its subset stamped with its OWN subscriptionId |
| Client cancels by closing the SSE stream (HTTP); stdio uses notifications/cancelled referencing the listen request id. | INFO | ✅ compliant | TESTED 2026-06-11: `dropping_one_subscription_leaves_others_delivering` (drop unregisters cleanly, siblings keep delivering) + `cancellation_2026.rs` (closing a request stream cancels its dispatch) |
| Server teardown (e.g. shutdown): "it MUST close the SSE stream (HTTP)". | MUST | 🟡 partial | Teardown today is socket-close (process exit closes every stream — the MUST is satisfied by the OS contract); there is no graceful in-process shutdown API (`McpServer::run` never returns), so an explicit-teardown test is not constructible. Dispositioned 2026-06-11: revisit only if a graceful-shutdown API is ever added |
| When the server tears a `subscriptions/listen` subscription down on its own initiative (e.g. shutdown), it SHOULD respond to the original request with an empty `SubscriptionsListenResult` before closing the stream, signaling a graceful end (see Graceful Closure). | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Folds two near-duplicate quotes (cancellation.mdx and subscriptions.mdx state the same SHOULD). `SubscriptionsListenResult`/`SubscriptionsListenResultMeta` are schema-bound and wire-tested (per OUTSTANDING.md lines 10, 32), but `handle_subscriptions_listen` has no shutdown-signal hook to emit the empty result from — no `CancellationToken`/`tokio::signal` wiring exists anywhere in the transport crates (OUTSTANDING.md line 57: "unbuilt infrastructure, not a missed call site"). Same root cause as the existing sibling row's 🟡 grade (server teardown today is bare process-exit socket-close). |
| On stdio reconnect the client MUST re-send subscriptions/listen — server holds no subscription state across reconnections. | MUST | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport in client or server on this branch (client transports: http.rs, sse.rs; server is HTTP-only). Server holds no cross-request subscription state by construction (per-request registry entry, streamable_http.rs:1930-1948) |
| A transport that closes without the graceful-end signal indicates an unexpected disconnect, which the client MAY treat as a trigger to reconnect. | MAY | ❌ gap | Not implemented: `SubscriptionStream` (`client.rs:2240-2249+`) and `subscriptions_listen()` (`client.rs:1117`) have no reconnect call site tied to abrupt stream closure — grep of `client.rs` for `reconnect`/`Reconnect` near subscription code finds only unrelated session-`-32031` reconnect logic (`client.rs:683-705`), not subscription-stream-close handling. |
| Notification filter shape: toolsListChanged/promptsListChanged/resourcesListChanged booleans + resourceSubscriptions string[]; all optional; omitted = not subscribed. | INFO | ✅ compliant | SubscriptionFilter in turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/subscriptions.rs:40-78; omitted/empty filter delivers nothing (registry entry created even when empty, streamable_http.rs:1930-1933); listen requires Accept: text/event-stream → 400 otherwise (subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_requires_sse_accept) |

## Cancellation

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/patterns/cancellation>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| notifications/cancelled shape: requestId (optional per schema) + optional reason string. | INFO | ✅ compliant | CancelledNotification/CancelledNotificationParams with Option<RequestId> + reason (turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/notifications.rs:311-358); tests notifications::tests::test_cancelled_notification_without_id, ::test_cancelled_notification_deserializes_without_request_id (COMPLIANCE.md:170) |
| "Streamable HTTP: Closing the SSE response stream is the cancellation signal. The server MUST treat a client disconnect as cancellation of that request." | MUST | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (982667fb, PAT/G1 + TX/GAP-2): streaming dispatch races `sender.closed()` — on disconnect the dispatch future is dropped (work stops at the next await), the progress task shuts down, nothing further is sent. Wire test `cancellation_2026.rs::client_disconnect_cancels_the_in_flight_request` (+ connected-path control) |
| Streamable HTTP: "No notifications/cancelled message is required or expected." stdio: client MUST send notifications/cancelled referencing the request ID. | MUST | ➖ n/a | Client cancels by dropping the in-flight future/stream (HTTP semantics); stdio arm n/a — no stdio transport ships (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/{http.rs,sse.rs} only) |
| "Cancellation notifications MUST only reference requests that were previously issued in the same direction [and] are believed to still be in-progress." | MUST | 🟡 partial | Client-emitted: none on Streamable HTTP — the client cancels by closing the stream (no send site in client.rs), which is the correct HTTP behavior (no client→server `notifications/cancelled` on this transport). Server-emitted: the server-sent form is stdio-only per the Subscriptions §Cancellation split and this framework ships no stdio server, so no server `notifications/cancelled` is emitted either. With no `notifications/cancelled` emitted in either direction on the shipped HTTP transport, this mis-scoping MUST binds no message to violate. |
| Receivers SHOULD stop processing the cancelled request, free resources, and not send a response. | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Disconnect path FIXED 2026-06-11 (982667fb): dropping the dispatch future stops processing and no response is sent. Remaining (PAT-section G1, P2): inbound `notifications/cancelled` is not a registered method on the 2026 path, so an explicit cancellation notification does not stop processing | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Row stated: 'inbound notifications/cancelled is not a registered method on the 2026 path, so an explicit cancellation notification does not stop processing'. Commit 71f323f1 (2026-06-11) 'test(server): subscriptions/cancellation P2 batch' explicitly registers notifications/cancelled in crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:1722 and builder.rs (added in diff). Test cancellation_2026.rs:133-16
| Receivers MAY ignore cancellation notifications when the request is unknown, already completed, or cannot be cancelled; "Invalid cancellation notifications SHOULD be ignored" (fire-and-forget, 202). | MAY | 🧪 untested | All notifications return 202 unconditionally and failures are logged, never errored to the client (streamable_http.rs:1806-1833). No test POSTs notifications/cancelled on the 2026 path (stateless_2026_http_surface.rs:226-250 covers notifications/progress only) |
| "The sender of the cancellation notification SHOULD ignore any response to the request that arrives afterward." | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | On HTTP, cancelling = dropping the response stream — a later response is physically unreadable (transport/http.rs streaming reader ends with the connection). No code path delivers a post-cancel response to the caller |
| "Both parties MUST handle these race conditions gracefully" (cancellation arriving after completion/response). | MUST | 🟡 partial | Server: failed wire send after disconnect breaks the forwarding loop harmlessly (streamable_http.rs:2213-2221); inbound late cancelled is ignored at 202. No test simulates disconnect-during-dispatch or late-cancel races on the 2026 path |
| "Both parties SHOULD log cancellation reasons for debugging"; application UIs SHOULD indicate cancellation requested. | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Inbound notifications/cancelled has no handler, so params.reason is never extracted/logged — only the generic 'Failed to process notification' error fires (streamable_http.rs:1815, handlers/mod.rs:1505-1508 logs raw params at info for listed methods only) — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12 (PAT/G10 fixed)**: dedicated `CancelledNotificationHandler` extracts `requestId` + `reason` into a structured info log ("Cancellation requested by peer"); accepted-and-ignored semantics unchanged (stream close remains the cancellation mechanism). UI indication is a host-application concern. |
| Server-initiated subscription teardown: the server **SHOULD** send the empty `subscriptions/listen` response (graceful closure) before closing the stream. (Per the Subscriptions §Cancellation split, the server-sent `notifications/cancelled` form is **stdio-only**; this framework ships no stdio server, and on Streamable HTTP teardown is closing the SSE stream.) | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | The `SubscriptionsListenResult` graceful-close type is bound and wire-tested (`subscriptions_listen_2026.rs`), but the server has no shutdown-signal hook (`CancellationToken`/`tokio::signal`) to emit it from — the SHOULD-level "sole survivor" item (OUTSTANDING.md). No `notifications/cancelled` send site exists, which is correct: the 2026 Streamable HTTP server does not emit that notification (it is stdio-only, and abrupt HTTP close carries no response, which is spec-legal). |
| Servers MUST NOT send `notifications/cancelled` for any other purpose. | MUST NOT | 🟡 partial | The 2026 Streamable HTTP server emits no `notifications/cancelled` at all — the server-sent form is stdio-only (not shipped here) — so it never sends one for a wrong purpose. Correct by construction on the shipped transport; untested only in that no stdio-server path exists to exercise the scope distinction. |
| "Implementations SHOULD establish timeouts for all sent requests, to prevent hung connections and resource exhaustion." | SHOULD | 🧪 untested | Client-side: `ClientConfig.timeouts.request` (`crates/turul-mcp-client/src/config.rs:98`) is a `Duration` applied via `tokio::time::timeout(...)` around every outbound request future (`client.rs:769-774` `send_request_with_headers_raw`, `:783-788` `send_request_raw`, `:1013-1019`, `:1945-1946`), erroring `McpClientError::Timeout` on elapse. No named test exercises this path — grep of `client.rs` finds no `#[test]`/`#[tokio::test]` asserting `McpClientError::Timeout`. Server-side (requests the server sends to the client, e.g. deprecated sampling/roots flows): not audited in this slice. |
| "When the request has not received a success or error response within the timeout period, the sender SHOULD cancel the request and stop waiting for a response." | SHOULD | 🧪 untested | Same `tokio::time::timeout` wrap as the row above: on elapse the wrapped future is dropped (ceasing to wait) and `McpClientError::Timeout` is returned instead of a response (`client.rs:769-774`, `:783-788`, `:1013-1019`). No named test asserts this specific stop-waiting/error-on-elapse behavior. |
| "Implementations MAY choose to reset the timeout clock when receiving a progress notification corresponding to the request, as this implies that work is actually happening." | MAY | ❌ gap | Not implemented: `config.timeouts.request` is a single fixed `Duration` passed straight to `tokio::time::timeout` (`client.rs:769,783,1013,1945`) with no progress-notification-triggered extension; grep of `client.rs` for `reset`/`extend` near `progress` finds no such logic. |
| "However, implementations SHOULD always enforce a maximum timeout, regardless of progress notifications, to limit the impact of a misbehaving client or server." | SHOULD | 🧪 untested | Trivially satisfied by the same fixed-duration design: since there is no progress-triggered reset (row above), `config.timeouts.request` already acts as an unconditional ceiling on every request (`client.rs:769,783,1013,1945`). No named test asserts the ceiling holds across intervening progress notifications specifically. |

## Transports — Overview

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/transports>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "JSON-RPC messages MUST be UTF-8 encoded" | MUST | 🧪 untested | crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1177-1187 rejects non-UTF-8 POST bodies with 400; no test sends a non-UTF-8 body — *Implemented; add a wire test posting invalid UTF-8* |
| "A binding MUST deliver client-sent requests and notifications to the server, and server-sent responses and notifications to the client" | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1733-1836 (request/notification dispatch); tests: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_call_dispatches_without_session_handshake, stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::responses_never_mint_session_ids (202 notification ack), subscriptions_listen_2026.rs (server notifications) |
| "A binding MAY additionally mirror selected body fields into envelope metadata... bindings that mirror metadata define how mismatches are rejected" | MAY | ✅ compliant | SEP-2243 mirroring + -32020 rejection: streamable_http.rs:1339-1467; constants crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/headers.rs:11-59; tests crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mcp_headers_2026.rs (7 tests) |
| Custom transports "MUST preserve the JSON-RPC message format, the message patterns, and the per-request metadata model" | MUST | ➖ n/a | No custom transport shipped — only Streamable HTTP; stdio is unimplemented (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport.rs:312-313 returns TransportError::Unsupported) |
| "Clients and servers **MAY** implement additional custom transport mechanisms to suit their specific needs" | MAY | ➖ n/a | Permissive antecedent for the two MUST/SHOULD custom-transport rows above; the framework exercises neither branch — only Streamable HTTP is shipped (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport.rs:312-313) |
| Custom transports "SHOULD document their connection establishment, message framing, and cancellation patterns"; byte-stream transports "SHOULD reuse the stdio framing" | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | No custom transport shipped (transport.rs:312-313) |
| Cancellation binding: "on Streamable HTTP it [the client] closes the request's response stream" (per-binding abandon mechanism) | INFO | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (982667fb): the server observes response-stream close and cancels the request (`cancellation_2026.rs`). Client-side, cancellation is performed by dropping the in-flight request/stream (no dedicated cancel() API; the wire test exercises exactly that drop) |
| Backward compatibility: "Clients and servers that interoperate with those [initialize-era] revisions detect the counterpart's era and fall back" per Versioning compat matrix | INFO | ✅ compliant | Client: probe_discover + classify_probe (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:272-385, src/version.rs:78-116; tests crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_locks_2026_when_server_answers_discover / ::bilingual_client_locks_2025_when_server_lacks_discover / ::bilingual_client_aborts_on_4xx_without_downgrade). 2025-lane server answers server/discover with -32601@200 as the trusted downgrade signal (streamable_http.rs:1551-1568) — *Fallback-to-2025 keys on exactly -32601 (plus -32004 as modern retry signal); a legacy server answering -32602 aborts instead of falling back — see GAP-6* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Row stated DEVIATION: 'Fallback-to-2025 keys on exactly -32601 (plus -32004 as modern retry signal); a legacy server answering -32602 aborts instead of falling back — see GAP-6'. Commit 839b122f (2026-06-11) 'feat(client): capability/discovery P2 batch' now treats both -32601 AND -32602 as legacy fallback signals per the spec: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/version.rs:89-95 now has 'for code in [-326

## Transports — stdio

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/transports/stdio>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Messages are delimited by newlines, and MUST NOT contain embedded newlines" | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport shipped on the 2026 branch: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport.rs:13-24 (module commented out) and :312-313 ('Stdio transport not yet implemented'); no stdio module exists in turul-mcp-server or turul-http-mcp-server |
