| "The server MAY write UTF-8 strings to stderr for any logging purposes"; "The client ... SHOULD NOT assume stderr output indicates error conditions" | MAY | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport shipped (transport.rs:312-313) |
| "The server MUST NOT write anything to its stdout that is not a valid MCP message" | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | No stdio server transport shipped |
| "The client MUST NOT write anything to the server's stdin that is not a valid MCP message"; "The client MUST NOT write JSON-RPC responses" | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | No stdio client transport shipped |
| "Clients MUST correlate these [listen-stream notifications] using the io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId field in _meta" | MUST | ➖ n/a | n/a on stdio (not shipped). HTTP equivalent is implemented and tested: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types asserts subscriptionId on every delivered notification |
| "The server MUST NOT write JSON-RPC requests to stdout. Server-to-client interactions are carried in InputRequiredResult replies" (MRTR) | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | n/a on stdio. HTTP equivalent compliant: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call / ::mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read |
| "The client MUST send a notifications/cancelled notification"; "Servers SHOULD stop work on a cancelled request ... and MUST NOT send any further messages for it" | MUST | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport shipped |
| "The client SHOULD initiate shutdown by: closing the input stream ... waiting ... forcibly terminating"; "Servers SHOULD exit promptly when their standard input is closed" | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport shipped |
| "The server **MAY** initiate shutdown by closing its output stream to the client and exiting" | MAY | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport shipped — no server-side stdout stream exists to close (no stdio module in turul-mcp-server or turul-http-mcp-server) |
| "If the server process exits unexpectedly, the client SHOULD restart it" and re-establish subscriptions/listen streams | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | No stdio transport shipped |
| Backward compat: dual-era client "SHOULD probe with server/discover before sending any other request"; "The fallback MUST NOT be keyed to one specific error code" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | n/a on stdio itself, but the same normative probe rule is implemented for HTTP: classify_probe (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/version.rs:78-116) probes first and never falls back to initialize on a bare 400 — however fallback-to-legacy fires ONLY on -32601 (METHOD_NOT_FOUND); -32602 from a legacy server → Abort — *See GAP-6: spec names -32601 OR -32602 as common legacy responses and forbids keying to one code* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Row stated: 'fallback-to-legacy fires ONLY on -32601 (METHOD_NOT_FOUND); -32602 from a legacy server → Abort — See GAP-6: spec names -32601 OR -32602'. Commit 839b122f fixed this: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/version.rs:94-95 now explicitly handles INVALID_PARAMS (-32602) the same as METHOD_NOT_FOUND (-32601), routing both to FallbackTo2025. The comment at line 89-93 explicitly cites the spec: 'leg
| "A client that only supports modern versions does not need to probe, but probing is still **RECOMMENDED**" | MAY | ➖ n/a | stdio-page clause, n/a (no stdio transport shipped). HTTP analog: crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:340 (`connect()`) calls `probe_discover`/`classify_probe` unconditionally on every connection — there is no config path that skips the probe for a modern-only client — but no row claims this HTTP behavior against this specific stdio-page sentence, so graded n/a rather than folding across pages |

## Transports — Streamable HTTP

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/transports/streamable-http>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "The server MUST provide a single HTTP endpoint path (the MCP endpoint) that supports POST" | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:543-563 POST routing on the /mcp path; every 2026 test suite exercises it (e.g. crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/stateless_2026_http_surface.rs) |
| "Servers MUST validate the Origin header on all incoming connections to prevent DNS rebinding attacks"; "If the Origin header is present and invalid, servers MUST respond with HTTP 403 Forbidden" | MUST | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (ADR-031): `OriginPolicy` on `ServerConfig` (default `SameOriginOrLoopback`; `AllowList`/`Disabled` knobs), enforced at both handler entries (`origin.rs::validate_origin`); wire tests `turul-mcp-server/tests/origin_validation_2026.rs` (7 tests, revert-and-fail proven) + 6 unit tests |
| "The HTTP response body **MAY** comprise a JSON-RPC _error response_ that has no `id`" (attached to the Origin-rejection 403) | MAY | ➖ n/a | Not exercised: the 403 body is a plain-text string, not a JSON-RPC error object (crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:528, `b"Forbidden: Origin not allowed"`). Spec-valid regardless — the clause is permissive (MAY), so omitting the optional JSON-RPC-error-body shape is not a violation; distinct from the notification-rejection 400 path (row above, which DOES send an id-less JSON-RPC error) |
| "When running locally, servers SHOULD bind only to localhost (127.0.0.1)" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Default bind address is 127.0.0.1:8000 — crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:256 and src/http.rs:30 |
| "Servers SHOULD implement proper authentication for all connections" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Opt-in OAuth 2.1 resource server (crates/turul-mcp-oauth) wired via pre-session middleware; 401 challenge path streamable_http.rs:1301-1312 + build_http_challenge_response:2775-2796 — *Opt-in, not default — acceptable for SHOULD* |
| "Every JSON-RPC message sent from the client MUST be a new HTTP POST request to the MCP endpoint" / "The client MUST use HTTP POST" | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/http.rs:650-880 — all sends are POSTs; e2e crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server |
| "The client MUST include an Accept header listing both application/json and text/event-stream" | MUST | ✅ compliant | MCP_POST_ACCEPT = "application/json, text/event-stream" (transport/http.rs:21, applied at :650/:766/:813/:870); test transport/http.rs:1388 test_accept_header_contains_both_media_types |
| "The client MUST include the request metadata headers on each POST request" (MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name) | MUST | ✅ compliant | apply_request_metadata_headers (transport/http.rs:220-242) applied at all 5 send sites incl. notifications (:653/:709/:768/:816/:874); MCP-Protocol-Version at :651 etc.; proven against an enforcing real server in e2e_2026_real_server.rs |
| "The body of the HTTP POST MUST be a single JSON-RPC request or notification. The client MUST NOT send JSON-RPC responses" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Server: parse_json_rpc_message admits only Request\|Notification (streamable_http.rs:1198); 2026 client never POSTs responses — server-to-client interactions re-issue the original call per MRTR (e2e_2026_real_server.rs::mrtr_round_trip_through_the_bilingual_client) |
| Notification accepted → "the server MUST return HTTP status code 202 Accepted with no body" | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1820-1834 (202, empty body, no session header); tests stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::responses_never_mint_session_ids, mcp_headers_2026.rs::notifications_also_require_mcp_method |
| Notification not acceptable → "it MUST return an HTTP error status code (e.g., 400 Bad Request)"; body MAY be an id-less JSON-RPC error | MUST | ✅ compliant | Header-validation failure on a notification → 400 with id-less HeaderMismatch (-32020) (streamable_http.rs:1441-1466, id=None for notifications); test mcp_headers_2026.rs::notifications_also_require_mcp_method (headerless → 400) |
| Request → "the server MUST return either Content-Type: application/json ... or Content-Type: text/event-stream ... The client MUST support both" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Server picks one of the two (streamable_http.rs:2110-2154 JSON inline path; :2427-2438 SSE path). Client parses both (transport/http.rs:371, :564 content-type branch); e2e tools/call exercises both framings |
| SSE stream: "The server MAY send JSON-RPC notifications ... before the final response. These notifications MUST relate to the originating client request" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Implemented: per-request progress channel registered before dispatch, scoped to the ephemeral per-request session (streamable_http.rs:2157-2283) so relation holds by construction; no 2026-lane test asserts mid-stream notifications on a POST SSE response — *GAP-5 coverage item* — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12**: request-scoping is wire-tested in crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/progress_2026.rs (`progress_echoes_the_request_string_token`, `progress_preserves_a_numeric_token`, `no_token_means_no_progress_notifications` — notifications appear only on the originating POST's SSE response and only with that request's token). |
| "Per the SSE specification, any line beginning with a colon is a comment that carries no event data; clients must ignore such lines and must not treat them as malformed input" | MUST | 🧪 implemented, untested | All three client SSE line-parsers only act on lines matching `data:`/`data: ` and silently `continue`/skip every other line via `strip_prefix` + `let-else` (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/http.rs:1301-1307 `parse_sse_lines`; :734 subscription stream; :1142-1143 event stream) — a `:`-prefixed comment line is ignored by construction, never producing a parse error. No test sends a comment line (`test_sse_data_field_without_space_after_colon` at :1775 tests a different case — a space-free `data:` prefix, not a colon-comment) |
| "The server MUST NOT send independent JSON-RPC requests on this stream. Server-to-client interactions ... are embedded as input requests inside an InputRequiredResult per MRTR" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | 2026 dispatch returns InputRequiredResult instead of server-initiated requests; tests crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call, ::mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read, ::undeclared_capability_is_rejected_with_32003 |
| "The final JSON-RPC response SHOULD terminate the stream" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Final frame sent then both senders dropped, closing the chunked body (streamable_http.rs:2340-2422); client e2e tools/call over SSE framing completes (e2e_2026_real_server.rs) |
| subscriptions/listen: response "is itself an SSE stream that stays open and delivers the change notifications the client opted in to"; request-scoped notifications are NOT delivered on the listen stream | MUST | ✅ compliant | handle_subscriptions_listen (streamable_http.rs:1850-2053): ack-first, honored-subset filter, per-URI resources/updated filter, subscriptionId stamping; only the four change-notification types pass the allow-list (:1933-1948, :1998-2015). Tests: subscriptions_listen_2026.rs (4 tests) + client e2e ::client_subscriptions_listen_receives_filtered_notifications |
| "When initiating an SSE stream, servers SHOULD include the X-Accel-Buffering: no header" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Present on subscriptions/listen (streamable_http.rs:2049) and legacy GET SSE (stream_manager.rs:779,856); MISSING on the per-request POST SSE response (create_streaming_response, streamable_http.rs:2433-2438 sets only Cache-Control/Transfer-Encoding) — *GAP-3* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:2518 (per-request POST SSE response) and line 2109 (subscriptions/listen) both include X-Accel-Buffering header. Commit 3ad11118 ('feat: server wire-edges P2 batch') fixed this gap.
| "Resumable SSE streams via Last-Event-ID are not supported" / legacy Last-Event-ID header → "ignore it; streams are not resumable" | MUST | ✅ compliant | GET (the only path that read Last-Event-ID) returns 405 under 2026 (streamable_http.rs:521-530); test stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::get_with_last_event_id_returns_405 |
| "Closing the SSE response stream MUST be treated by the server as cancellation of that request. The server SHOULD stop work ... and MUST NOT send any further messages for it" | MUST | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (982667fb): dispatch raced against the response channel's `closed()` signal — disconnect drops the dispatch future (stops work), shuts down the progress task, and sends nothing further. Red phase recorded (the detached task previously ran to completion); wire test `cancellation_2026.rs` |
| "Every POST request to the MCP endpoint MUST include an MCP-Protocol-Version header"; server that supports no pre-2025-06-18 clients "MUST reject a request without the header" | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1391-1392 (absent header → 400 -32020); client emits it on every send (transport/http.rs:651 etc.); test mcp_headers_2026.rs::missing_protocol_version_header_is_rejected |
| "The header value MUST match the io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion field carried in the request body's _meta. If the values do not match, the server MUST reject the request with 400 Bad Request and a HeaderMismatch JSON-RPC error" | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1656-1712 (mismatch → ERROR_CODE_HEADER_MISMATCH, -32020, @ 400 — the row's prior -32001 citation was stale; -32001 is the unrelated legacy 2025-11-25 missing-session-id code at streamable_http.rs:1632); test discover_stateless_2026.rs::header_body_protocol_version_mismatch_is_rejected_with_32020 |
| "If the server does not implement the requested protocol version ... it MUST respond with 400 Bad Request and an UnsupportedProtocolVersionError listing its supported versions" | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1361-1389 (-32022 with supported list, HTTP 400; code defined at crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/lib.rs:482-486); test discover_stateless_2026.rs::unsupported_protocol_version_header_is_rejected_with_32022 |
| "If the server does not implement the requested RPC method, it MUST respond with 404 Not Found and a JSON-RPC error with code -32601" | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1683-1705; tests crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/error_mapping_2026.rs::unknown_method_gets_http_404_with_method_not_found, ::methods_absent_from_the_2026_schema_get_404 (ping/initialize/tasks/*/logging/setLevel/resources/subscribe), ::known_methods_are_unaffected |
| Server "MAY treat a request that omits the header as protocol version 2025-03-26" (only when supporting pre-2025-06-18 clients) | MAY | ➖ n/a | The 2026 build declares it supports no pre-2025-06-18 clients and takes the reject branch instead (streamable_http.rs:1339-1348 comment + :1391); permitted by the spec's either/or |
| "Mcp-Method ... All requests and notifications" and "Mcp-Name ... tools/call, resources/read, prompts/get" — "These headers are REQUIRED for compliance" and MUST match the body | MUST | 🟡 partial | streamable_http.rs:1394-1439 (Mcp-Method presence+match for requests AND notifications; Mcp-Name vs params.name for tools/call+prompts/get, params.uri for resources/read); constants headers.rs:15-27. Tests mcp_headers_2026.rs cover tools/call (missing/mismatch), resources/read (uri match+mismatch), notifications, and no-name methods — but NOT the prompts/get Mcp-Name path — *GAP-4: prompts/get branch implemented but untested* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Code at crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1461 handles prompts/get Mcp-Name validation alongside tools/call and resources/read. However, crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mcp_headers_2026.rs still lacks a test exercising the prompts/get path (comment at line 9 says it should be tested, but no test exists). Code is compliant; test coverage is missing. Suggest 🟡.
| x-mcp-header: "servers MAY designate specific tool parameters to be mirrored"; "clients MUST support this feature" and "MUST mirror the designated parameter values into HTTP headers" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Server annotation scan: headers.rs::scan_x_mcp_headers (:110-181). Client mirroring: collect_param_bindings + encode_param_headers (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:105-140; client.rs:906-922, cached_param_bindings client.rs:55-60); proven end-to-end against a validating server: e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_mirrors_mcp_param_headers_from_x_mcp_header_annotations |
| x-mcp-header value constraints: non-empty, RFC 9110 tchar syntax, no control chars, case-insensitively unique, primitive types only (no number), integer in JS safe range, any nesting depth | MUST | ✅ compliant | scan_x_mcp_headers enforces tchar/empty/type/uniqueness/nesting (headers.rs:110-181); encode_param_value enforces safe-range integers and rejects floats (headers.rs:191-218). Tests: headers.rs param_tests::scan_finds_annotations_and_enforces_constraints, ::integer_and_boolean_conversion |
| x-mcp-header "MUST only be applied to properties that are statically reachable from the schema root ... MUST NOT pass through `items` ... composition keywords ... conditional keywords ... or `$ref`" | MUST | 🧪 implemented, untested | `scan_x_mcp_headers::walk` (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/headers.rs:115-163) only recurses via a property's own `properties` map — it never inspects `items`, `oneOf`/`anyOf`/`allOf`/`not`, `if`/`then`/`else`, or `$ref`, so the exclusion holds by construction. No test schema exercises an `x-mcp-header` nested inside any of those keywords to prove it is silently ignored rather than accidentally picked up |
| "Clients using the Streamable HTTP transport MUST reject tool definitions where any x-mcp-header value violates these constraints ... MUST exclude the invalid tool from the result of tools/list. Clients SHOULD log a warning" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Implemented at `parse_list_tools` (scan_x_mcp_headers filter + tracing::warn); TESTED 2026-06-11 (3c73c6c3, TOOLS-G1): `bilingual_2026_operations.rs::invalid_x_mcp_header_tools_are_excluded_from_tools_list` (invalid tool excluded, valid tool survives; revert-and-fail proven) |
| Value encoding: type conversion rules; "clients MUST use Base64 encoding ... =?base64?{value}?=" for non-ASCII/control/padded values; "clients MUST also Base64-encode any plain-ASCII value that matches the sentinel pattern"; markers case-sensitive lowercase | MUST | ✅ compliant | encode_param_value (headers.rs:191-218) implements all five spec examples; tests headers.rs param_tests::encoding_matches_the_spec_examples (exact spec table values), ::decode_round_trips; sentinel constants tested header/base64_sentinel_exact_spelling |
| "If the `Mcp-Name` source value cannot be safely represented as a plain ASCII header value, clients **MUST** encode it using the Base64 sentinel format" | MUST | ✅ compliant | **FIXED 2026-07-14**: `apply_request_metadata_headers` now routes the `Mcp-Name` value through `encode_param_value` (Base64 sentinel when not safely plain ASCII, verbatim otherwise), cfg-gated to the lanes that link the 2026 protocol crate — the 2025-11-25-only lane never reaches the 2026 header path at runtime. Wire test: bilingual_negotiation.rs::mcp_name_header_is_base64_sentinel_encoded_when_not_plain_ascii (mock matches ONLY the encoded form `=?base64?IHBhZGRlZCA=?=`; revert-and-fail recorded) |
| "Servers and intermediaries that need to inspect these values MUST decode them accordingly"; "Any server that processes the message body MUST validate that encoded header values, after decoding ... match the corresponding values in the request body. Servers MUST reject ... with 400 ... HeaderMismatch (-32020)" (incl. omit-header rules for null/absent params, reject when header omitted but value in body) | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:1920-1999: decode via decode_param_value, full presence/absence matrix (:1950-1963), mismatch → ERROR_CODE_HEADER_MISMATCH (-32020) @ 400. Tests crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mcp_param_2026.rs::matching_param_header_passes_validation, ::base64_encoded_param_header_decodes_and_matches, ::omitted_param_header_with_body_value_is_rejected, ::mismatched_param_header_is_rejected |
| "servers SHOULD compare the header value and the body value numerically rather than as strings (e.g., 42.0 and 42 are considered equal)" for integer parameters | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | TESTED 2026-06-11: `mcp_param_2026.rs::integer_params_compare_numerically` — "42.0" header vs 42 body accepted, true mismatch → -32020/400 |
| Client without the tool's inputSchema "SHOULD send the request without custom Mcp-Param-* headers. If the server rejects ... the client SHOULD call tools/list ... then retry" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | mcp_param_headers_for returns empty on cache miss (client.rs:906-922); on a HeaderMismatch (-32020) rejection the client refreshes tools/list once and retries with recomputed headers (client.rs:1062-1074, fixed in commit 4690e007). Test: bilingual_negotiation.rs::call_tool_recovers_from_header_mismatch_with_one_refresh_and_retry |
| "Clients **MUST** construct `Mcp-Param-*` headers using the most recently obtained `inputSchema` for the tool" | MUST | ✅ compliant | `cached_param_bindings` (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:59-60) is populated from `tools/list` responses (:902) and invalidated on `notifications/tools/list_changed` (:243-248); on a HeaderMismatch (-32020) response the client refreshes `tools/list` once and retries (:1062-1074). Test: crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs::call_tool_recovers_from_header_mismatch_with_one_refresh_and_retry (fixed in 4690e007) |
| "Clients **MAY** pre-load tool definitions via other means (e.g., from a previous session or configuration) to enable header emission without a prior `tools/list` call" | MAY | ❌ gap | `cached_param_bindings` (client.rs:59-60) has no public seeding API — it is populated only as a side effect of an internal `tools/list` call (client.rs:902); a caller cannot pre-load bindings from a prior session or static config to enable header emission on a cold client |
| "Intermediate servers that do not recognize an Mcp-Param-{Name} header MUST forward it and otherwise ignore it" | MUST | ➖ n/a | The framework ships no HTTP intermediary role |
| "Intermediaries MUST return an appropriate HTTP error status (e.g., 400 Bad Request) for validation failures but are not required to return a JSON-RPC error response" | MUST | ➖ n/a | The framework ships no HTTP intermediary/proxy role — only an origin server and a client (same disposition as the adjacent Mcp-Param forwarding row) |
| "Intermediaries that enforce policy based on mirrored headers ... SHOULD verify that the MCP-Protocol-Version header indicates a version that requires header–body validation. If the version is older or the header is absent, the intermediary SHOULD reject the request rather than trusting unvalidated header values" | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | The framework ships no HTTP intermediary/proxy role — both sentences describe intermediary-only policy enforcement that has no corresponding component in this codebase |
| "Clients and servers MUST use case-insensitive comparisons for header names"; header values are case-sensitive | MUST | ✅ compliant | Server reads hyper-lowercased header map with lowercase keys (streamable_http.rs:1346-1355, server.rs:1945 lowercased mcp-param keys); values compared exactly (mcp_headers_2026.rs mismatch tests prove value case-sensitivity indirectly) |
| Server Validation: "servers MUST reject requests where the values specified in the headers do not match the corresponding values in the request body" → "MUST return HTTP status 400 ... and MUST include a JSON-RPC error response using ... HeaderMismatch (-32020)" (missing/mismatched/invalid-char headers) | MUST | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:1441-1466 + ERROR_CODE_HEADER_MISMATCH (headers.rs:58-59, value -32020, tested in-range); wire tests mcp_headers_2026.rs (all assert 400 + -32020) and mcp_param_2026.rs |
| "In particular, servers MUST decode an encoded `Mcp-Name` or `Mcp-Param-{Name}` value before comparing it to the corresponding request body value during Server Validation" | MUST | ✅ compliant | **RE-GRADED 2026-07-13 (B4 fixed)**: both halves now decode before comparing. `Mcp-Param-{Name}`: server.rs:1920-1999 via `decode_param_value` (tests mcp_param_2026.rs). `Mcp-Name`: streamable_http.rs:1474-1494 now routes through the same `decode_param_value` path (including its decode-failure semantics), closing the gap where a Base64-sentinel-encoded `Mcp-Name` was falsely rejected as a mismatch. Tests: mcp_headers_2026.rs::base64_encoded_mcp_name_decodes_and_matches (revert-and-fail recorded), ::base64_encoded_mcp_name_mismatch_is_rejected (-32020) |
| Backward compat: dual-era client "MAY detect which era the server implements by attempting a modern request first. On 400 ... SHOULD inspect the response body before falling back"; recognized modern error → retry/correct, never fall back; unrecognized → fall back to initialize | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | probe_discover inspects 400 bodies for modern JSON-RPC errors (client.rs:374-383); classify_probe: -32601→fallback, -32022→fallback, recognized-but-other modern error→no downgrade (version.rs:78-116); tests bilingual_negotiation.rs (4 tests) incl. ::bilingual_client_aborts_on_4xx_without_downgrade — *Deviation: bare 4xx without modern error body ABORTS by default instead of falling back to initialize (opt-in allow_legacy_gateway_fallback for 404/405) — deliberate anti-downgrade posture, stricter than the SHOULD; and fallback keys on -32601 only (see GAP-6)* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-client/src/version.rs:94-95 show both METHOD_NOT_FOUND (-32601) and INVALID_PARAMS (-32602) map to ProbeDecision::FallbackTo2025, fixing the gap about keying to 'one specific error code'. Tests in crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs cover the fallback paths. Commit 839b122f ('feat(client): capability/discovery P2 batch') addressed this.
| 2026-only server receiving legacy traffic: "HTTP GET or DELETE to the MCP endpoint: respond with 405 Method Not Allowed" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | streamable_http.rs:521-530 (405 + Allow: POST, OPTIONS, gated ahead of Accept/session validation); tests stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::get_returns_405_method_not_allowed, ::delete_returns_405_method_not_allowed |
| "An Mcp-Session-Id header on a request: ignore it, and do not mint or echo session IDs" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Inbound header discarded at parse (streamable_http.rs:188-189); ephemeral internal session never written to context or response (:1707-1728, :1820-1827); tests stateless_2026_http_surface.rs::inbound_mcp_session_id_is_ignored_and_never_echoed, ::responses_never_mint_session_ids |
| HTTP+SSE (2024-11-05) transport: "New implementations SHOULD NOT adopt it; existing implementations SHOULD migrate to Streamable HTTP" | SHOULD NOT | ✅ compliant | The 2026 build hosts only the Streamable HTTP POST endpoint; the legacy session_handler.rs path is part of the 2025-11-25 lane, not the 2026 surface (GET/DELETE 405 tests prove the old transport is not reachable) |
| (brief item, body-level not on this page) list/read results carry ttlMs/cacheScope (CacheableResult) | INFO | ✅ compliant | tests discover_stateless_2026.rs::resources_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result, ::prompts_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result; DiscoverResult extends CacheableResult per ADR-027 revision 2026-06-07 — *Caching is a schema contract, not a transports-page requirement; audited fully in the schema section* |

## MCP draft Basic: Authorization (2026-07-28 draft)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/authorization>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Authorization is OPTIONAL; HTTP-based implementations "SHOULD conform to this specification" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Opt-in RS support: crates/turul-mcp-oauth (ADR-021 RS-only scope); wired via pre-session middleware crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1271-1337 — *Server-side RS role only; AS and client OAuth-flow roles not shipped (ADR-022 non-goals)* |
| STDIO transports "SHOULD NOT follow this specification" — credentials from environment | SHOULD NOT | ➖ n/a | Client stdio transport carries no OAuth flow; tokens are caller-provided env-side. No stdio OAuth surface exists to violate this |
| Authorization servers MUST implement OAuth 2.1 with appropriate security measures | MUST | ➖ n/a | No AS shipped — docs/adr/021-oauth-resource-server-architecture.md "Scope Decision: Resource Server Only"; tokens issued by external AS, validated via JWKS |
| Authorization servers and MCP clients SHOULD support Client ID Metadata Documents (CIMD) | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Brief's question verified: the page assigns CIMD to AS+clients only — NO RS-side CIMD duty. Posture documented in crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/lib.rs:17-27; wire-shape pin test metadata.rs::protected_resource_metadata_carries_no_client_registration_surface (metadata.rs:216-253) — *turul-mcp-client ships no OAuth client flow (ADR-022 §Non-goals), so the client-side SHOULD is out of shipped scope — see gap AUTH-4* |
| Authorization-server-side CIMD handling: SHOULD fetch metadata documents for URL-formatted client_ids; MUST validate the fetched `client_id` matches the URL exactly; SHOULD cache respecting HTTP cache headers; MUST validate redirect URIs and JSON structure; SHOULD follow CIMD security-considerations guidance; SHOULD consider SSRF risk when fetching | SHOULD/MUST | ➖ n/a | No authorization server is shipped (docs/adr/021-oauth-resource-server-architecture.md, RS-only scope) — turul-mcp-oauth never fetches a CIMD document, so this AS-role fetch/validate/cache/SSRF surface does not exist. Distinct from the client-side SSRF n/a already in the Security Best Practices section (that covers client-fetched OAuth URLs; this is AS-fetched CIMD documents) — folds 7 quotes from basic/authorization/client-registration.mdx
| Implementations using alternative (non-HTTP, non-STDIO) transports MUST follow established security best practices for their protocol | MUST | ➖ n/a | Framework ships only HTTP (Streamable HTTP) and STDIO transports (crates/turul-http-mcp-server, turul-mcp-server stdio binding) — no third transport exists to bear this requirement |
| AS and MCP clients MAY support Dynamic Client Registration (RFC 7591, deprecated) | MAY | ➖ n/a | AS/client concern; crate has never implemented DCR (lib.rs:24-27). Same wire-shape test pins no registration keys in RFC 9728 doc |
| MCP servers MUST implement OAuth 2.0 Protected Resource Metadata (RFC 9728) | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/metadata.rs:80-177 (validated doc, root+path well-known forms per RFC 9728 §3), well_known.rs:31-44, lib.rs:100-126; tests metadata.rs::test_metadata_json_matches_rfc9728, ::test_well_known_paths, well_known.rs::test_well_known_endpoint_returns_metadata, ::test_path_form_endpoint_returns_same_metadata |
| MCP servers MUST implement one of: (1) WWW-Authenticate header carrying `resource_metadata` on 401, or (2) well-known URI metadata document, to give clients authorization-server location | MUST | ✅ compliant | Both mechanisms are implemented: (1) crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/middleware.rs:49-61 `build_challenge` embeds `resource_metadata=` on every 401, tested middleware.rs::test_www_authenticate_contains_resource_metadata_url; (2) well_known.rs:31-44 serves root and path-scoped `.well-known/oauth-protected-resource[/{path}]`, tested well_known.rs::test_well_known_endpoint_returns_metadata, ::test_path_form_endpoint_returns_same_metadata — server ships both, so this MUST is satisfied regardless of which the spec ultimately requires
| The Protected Resource Metadata document MUST include `authorization_servers` containing at least one authorization server | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/metadata.rs:86-91 (`ProtectedResourceMetadata::new` rejects an empty `authorization_servers` vec at construction); test metadata.rs::test_reject_empty_authorization_servers
| MCP clients MUST use Protected Resource Metadata for authorization server discovery | MUST | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client performs no AS discovery — no resource_metadata/well-known fetch code (grep over crates/turul-mcp-client/src is doc-comment-only hits in client.rs:424, transport.rs:176). Token acquisition is the embedding app's duty (ADR-022 §Non-goals) — *Becomes a gap the moment the client claims MCP auth support — gap AUTH-4* |
| MCP AS MUST provide RFC 8414 or OIDC Discovery; clients MUST support both discovery mechanisms | MUST | ➖ n/a | AS side: no AS shipped. Client side: no discovery flow shipped (same disposition as PRM discovery row) |
| Clients MUST obtain a client ID via CIMD, pre-registration, or DCR per the Client Registration sub-page | MUST | ➖ n/a | No client registration surface in turul-mcp-client; bearer is injected by the caller (transport/http.rs:42-46 auth override) |
| Client Registration sub-page normative detail: 4-step selection priority (pre-registered → CIMD → DCR → user prompt); CIMD hosting (HTTPS URL, path-bearing `client_id`, required `client_id`/`client_name`/`redirect_uris` properties, `client_id` MUST match document URL); `private_key_jwt` support (MAY); native-vs-web `application_type` classification (SHOULD); registration-failure handling, error surfacing, retry, and mismatched-credential detection (SHOULD/MUST) | MUST/SHOULD/MAY | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client ships no client-registration surface of any kind — no CIMD document hosting, no DCR, no `application_type` negotiation, no registration-failure handling (bearer is injected directly by the caller via `set_bearer`, transport/http.rs:42-46). Same client-role disposition as the existing Client Registration n/a row (416/419), now covering the granular normative detail from basic/authorization/client-registration.mdx — folds 13 quotes from that sub-page (gap AUTH-4 precondition)
| MCP servers SHOULD include a `scope` parameter in the WWW-Authenticate header (RFC 6750 §3) | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/middleware.rs:40-53 (build_challenge); tests middleware.rs::test_401_includes_scope_when_configured, ::test_401_omits_scope_when_not_configured — *Emitted only when scopes_supported is configured (ADR-021 D9)* |
| Implementations SHOULD consistently use the resource-URI form without a trailing slash for interoperability, unless the trailing slash is semantically significant | SHOULD | ❌ gap | No normalization exists — `validate_canonical_uri` (crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/metadata.rs:47-71) checks scheme/fragment only, never strips or rejects a trailing slash; the resource URI is passed through verbatim from operator config. No test asserts trailing-slash handling either way
| WWW-Authenticate challenge scopes MAY match, subset, superset, or be unrelated to `scopes_supported`; servers SHOULD strive for scope-set consistency but need not surface every dynamically issued scope; SHOULD weigh UX impact of scopes returned | MAY/SHOULD | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/middleware.rs:49-57 `build_challenge` always echoes the operator-configured `scopes_supported` verbatim into the `scope` param — one of the MAY-permitted set relationships (exact match), trivially consistent since scopes are static config, not dynamically issued; tests middleware.rs::test_401_includes_scope_when_configured, ::test_401_omits_scope_when_not_configured — *No dynamic/per-request scope issuance exists in this RS-only crate (ADR-021), so the 'not required to surface every dynamically issued scope' and UX-impact clauses have no decision surface to violate — folds 3 quotes from basic/authorization/index.mdx*
| Clients MUST NOT assume a set relationship between challenge scopes and scopes_supported; MUST treat challenge scopes as authoritative; SHOULD union with previously granted scopes on re-auth | MUST | ➖ n/a | Client-flow requirement; no scope handling exists in turul-mcp-client |
| Clients SHOULD follow least privilege and the scope-selection priority order (WWW-Authenticate scope, else scopes_supported) | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Client-flow requirement; not shipped (gap AUTH-4 if claiming client auth) |
| Client MUST record the issuer from validated AS metadata before redirecting, bound to the PKCE record (RFC 9207 prerequisite) | MUST | ➖ n/a | No authorization-code flow in turul-mcp-client — no redirect, no PKCE, no issuer record. Prior audit §4.4 flags RFC 9207 as a precondition for future client auth claims |
| Client-side authorization-server discovery mechanics: parse `WWW-Authenticate` and respond to 401s; fall back to well-known URIs in priority order for path/no-path issuers; validate the metadata document's `issuer` matches the URL used to fetch it and MUST NOT use mismatched (e.g. attacker-supplied) metadata | MUST/MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client performs no AS discovery of any kind (client.rs:424, transport.rs:176 are doc-comment-only hits, no fetch code) — same disposition as the existing PRM-discovery n/a row, now extended to the specific well-known-priority-order and issuer-confusion/SSRF mechanics from basic/authorization/authorization-server-discovery.mdx (gap AUTH-4 if client auth is ever claimed) — folds 7 quotes from that sub-page
| AS SHOULD include `iss` in authorization responses; AS including it MUST advertise authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported=true | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | AS-role requirement; no AS shipped (ADR-021). Note: distinct from the RS-side JWT `iss` claim check, which IS implemented — jwt.rs:159-161,174 + test test_wrong_issuer_rejected |
| Clients MUST apply the RFC 9207 §2.4 iss-validation table before sending the code to any token endpoint; MUST NOT normalize iss before comparison; on mismatch MUST NOT act on error/error_description/error_uri | MUST | ➖ n/a | Client authorization-response handling does not exist in turul-mcp-client (no auth-code flow) |
| Clients MUST implement RFC 8707 resource indicators: in both authorization and token requests, identifying the MCP server by canonical URI, sent regardless of AS support | MUST | ➖ n/a | No token request is ever constructed by turul-mcp-client; the caller supplies the finished bearer (client.rs::set_bearer, transport/http.rs:1281) — *Gap AUTH-4 covers the missing client auth flow as a precondition for compliance claims* |
| Clients SHOULD provide the most specific canonical URI; implementations SHOULD accept uppercase scheme/host | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Same disposition — no resource parameter emitted anywhere |
| MCP client MUST use the Authorization: Bearer header; authorization MUST be included in every HTTP request from client to server | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/http.rs:244-247 (apply_auth_override supersedes default_headers), wired into all five outbound surfaces (send_request, send_request_with_headers, send_notification, send_delete, SSE GET — http.rs:930, 1026); tests crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/wire_compliance.rs::test_send_request_uses_overridden_bearer_after_rotation, ::test_send_delete_uses_overridden_bearer_after_rotation (real reqwest pipeline vs wiremock) — *Conditional on the caller configuring a bearer — the client cannot mint one itself* |
| Access tokens MUST NOT be included in the URI query string | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Bearer flows exclusively via RequestBuilder::header (transport/http.rs:244-247); no code path appends tokens to URLs. Wire tests above assert header placement; no test asserts query-string absence (structural compliance) |
| MCP servers MUST validate access tokens per OAuth 2.1 §5.2 | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/jwt.rs:124-179 (signature via JWKS, exp, alg allow-list + JWKS alg cross-check, iss, aud); tests jwt.rs::test_valid_jwt_accepted, ::test_expired_jwt_rejected_401, ::test_alg_none_rejected |
| MCP servers MUST validate that tokens were issued specifically for them as intended audience (RFC 8707 §2) | MUST | ✅ compliant | Audience is a required constructor param with no opt-out — jwt.rs:93-103, validation at jwt.rs:163-167; lib.rs:111-113 auto-wires audience=resource; tests jwt.rs::test_audience_always_validated, ::test_wrong_audience_rejected (ADR-021 D7) |
| If validation fails, servers MUST respond per OAuth 2.1 §5.3; invalid or expired tokens MUST receive HTTP 401 | MUST | ✅ compliant | middleware.rs 401 + error="invalid_token"; transport `build_http_challenge_response` (WWW-Authenticate, Cache-Control: no-store); TESTED 2026-06-11 (134703e9, AUTH-1): real-HTTP `oauth_2026.rs` — missing/garbage bearer → 401 challenge with resource_metadata, 401 outranks the missing-_meta 400, RFC 9728 well-known routes public (revert-and-fail proven) |
| MCP clients MUST NOT send tokens to the MCP server other than ones issued by the MCP server's authorization server | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | The client never selects or acquires tokens — the embedding application owns token provenance (set_bearer API). Unenforceable at the library layer without the discovery flow |
| MCP servers MUST only accept tokens valid for their own resources; MUST NOT accept or transit any other tokens | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Accept: mandatory audience binding (jwt.rs:163-167, test test_audience_always_validated). Transit: raw bearer never reaches tools or metadata — D5 isolation at streamable_http.rs:1288-1293/session_handler.rs:405-409; only validated claims are injected (middleware.rs:85-89) — *No dedicated test asserts the raw token is absent from SessionContext extensions* |
| Refresh tokens: clients MUST keep them confidential; SHOULD declare refresh_token grant; MAY request offline_access; MUST NOT assume issuance | MUST | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client has no refresh-token handling of any kind (no token endpoint interaction) |
| MCP Servers SHOULD NOT include offline_access in WWW-Authenticate scope or PRM scopes_supported | SHOULD NOT | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (AUTH-5): `ProtectedResourceMetadata::with_scopes` filters `offline_access` with a warning before it can reach scopes_supported or the WWW-Authenticate scope param; unit-tested |
| Servers MUST return appropriate HTTP status codes: 401 unauthorized/invalid token, 403 insufficient scope, 400 malformed authorization request | MUST | ✅ compliant | 401 path compliant (above). 403: HttpChallenge supports status 403 + Cache-Control no-store (middleware/error.rs:114-121, streamable_http.rs:2775-2796) but nothing shipped emits it. 400: malformed Bearer values are rejected by the hardened parser (middleware/bearer.rs:22) and then treated as MISSING → 401 challenge without error param, not 400 invalid_request — *Gaps AUTH-2, AUTH-3* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1748-1752 now check if method=='initialize' and add error.data.supported field with the server's supported protocol versions. This directly addresses the spec requirement that initialize errors SHOULD name the supported versions. Commit 3ad11118 ('feat: server wire-edges P2 batch') fixed this.
| On insufficient scope the server SHOULD respond 403 + WWW-Authenticate with error="insufficient_scope", scope, resource_metadata; SHOULD include all scopes for the operation in a single challenge; SHOULD be consistent | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (AUTH-2): `with_required_scopes` on the middleware — missing scope → 403 + WWW-Authenticate with error="insufficient_scope" and the scope list (pre-session phase, same challenge transport as the wire-tested 401); unit tests `insufficient_scope_returns_403_challenge` / `sufficient_scope_passes_and_injects_claims` |
| Clients SHOULD respond to scope errors with step-up authorization (union of prior + challenged scopes), SHOULD implement retry limits and track upgrade attempts | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Client-flow requirement; no scope/step-up machinery in turul-mcp-client. Client treats 4xx on SSE GET as terminal (tests/sse_terminal_4xx.rs) — orthogonal to step-up |
| Implementations MUST follow the normative security requirements in the Security Considerations sub-page (audience binding, token theft, communication security, code protection, mix-up/confused deputy, open redirection, CIMD security) | MUST | 🟡 partial | RS-relevant items verified compliant here (audience binding D7, comm security via operator TLS). Sub-page (/specification/draft/basic/authorization/security-considerations) not in this audit's URL set — client/AS items n/a by role — *The draft restructured authorization into sub-pages (discovery, client-registration, security-considerations) carrying normative text; a follow-up section audit should fetch them* |
| Client-side authorization-code-flow security requirements: MUST implement and verify PKCE (S256 when capable), MUST refuse to proceed if `code_challenge_methods_supported` is absent from AS/OIDC metadata; MUST have redirect URIs registered with the AS; SHOULD use and verify `state` parameters, discarding mismatches; MUST follow OAuth 2.1 §1.5 communication-security guidance | MUST/SHOULD | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client implements no authorization-code flow — no PKCE generation/verification, no redirect handling, no `state` parameter, no AS-metadata capability check exist anywhere in the crate (bearer is supplied pre-formed via `set_bearer`). Same disposition as the existing 'no authorization-code flow' n/a rows in this section — folds 6 quotes from basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx (gap AUTH-4 precondition)
| Authorization-server-side security requirements: SHOULD issue short-lived access tokens; MUST rotate refresh tokens for public clients; MUST serve all AS endpoints over HTTPS; MUST validate exact redirect URIs against pre-registered values; MUST take precautions against untrusted redirect targets; SHOULD only auto-redirect to trusted URIs; MAY implement domain-trust policies, attestation, or extra localhost-redirect warnings | MUST/SHOULD/MAY | ➖ n/a | No authorization server is shipped (ADR-021 RS-only scope) — token issuance, refresh-token lifecycle, and redirect-URI enforcement are all AS responsibilities with no corresponding code in turul-mcp-oauth or anywhere else in the workspace — folds 9 quotes from basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx
| Security Considerations sub-page: implementers MUST consider these requirements when building clients/servers; MUST follow OAuth 2.1 §7 best practices; clients and servers MUST implement secure token storage (OAuth 2.1 §7.1) | MUST | ➖ n/a | RS-relevant subset (audience binding, comms security) is already graded compliant elsewhere in this section. The token-storage clause has no surface in turul-mcp-oauth: the RS validates a bearer per-request via JWKS and never persists it (jwt.rs — no token cache/store); turul-mcp-client's caller-supplied bearer is likewise not persisted by the library beyond the in-memory override (transport/http.rs `apply_auth_override`). No storage-at-rest exists to secure or fail to secure — folds 3 quotes from basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx, refining the existing 🟡-partial 'security considerations sub-page' row rather than replacing it

## MCP draft Basic: Security Best Practices

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/basic/security_best_practices>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Confused deputy: MCP proxy servers MUST implement per-client consent (consent registry, consent UI, __Host- cookies, exact redirect_uri matching, state-parameter lifecycle) before forwarding to third-party authorization | MUST | ➖ n/a | Framework ships no MCP proxy server / OAuth-proxy role: turul-mcp-oauth is validate-only RS (lib.rs:1-27); no third-party AS delegation, no /authorize or /register endpoints exist anywhere in the workspace |
| Token passthrough: MCP servers MUST NOT accept any tokens that were not explicitly issued for the MCP server | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Mandatory audience validation, no opt-out — jwt.rs:93-103 + 163-167; tests jwt.rs::test_audience_always_validated, ::test_wrong_audience_rejected; raw bearer never forwarded downstream (D5: streamable_http.rs:1288-1293) |
| SSRF: MCP clients deployed to a server MUST consider SSRF risks and implement appropriate mitigations when fetching OAuth-related URLs | MUST | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client fetches no OAuth URLs (no resource_metadata / AS-metadata / token-endpoint fetch code) — the attack surface does not exist. Server-side JwtValidator fetches only the operator-configured jwks_uri (jwt.rs:208-232), not attacker-controlled input — *Must be revisited when the client auth flow lands (gap AUTH-4): discovery introduces exactly these attacker-controlled URLs* |
| SSRF: clients SHOULD require HTTPS for OAuth URLs (loopback exempt); SHOULD block private/reserved IP ranges (RFC 9728 §7.7); SHOULD validate redirect targets; operators SHOULD use egress proxies | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Same disposition — no OAuth URL fetching in the client |
| Session hijacking: MCP servers that implement authorization MUST verify all inbound requests; MUST NOT use sessions for authentication | MUST | 🟡 partial | 2026 default path: pre-session auth runs on every POST before header validation, no bypass (streamable_http.rs:1232-1233 hard-disables sessionless ping under protocol-2026-07-28; 1271-1337 auth phase). Auth is request-scoped, never session-persisted (ADR-021 D2; middleware.rs:85-89 writes claims to per-request extensions only). 2025 opt-in lane: sessionless ping with allow_unauthenticated_ping=true (default) returns/dispatches BEFORE execute_before_session (streamable_http.rs:~1226-1268 precedes 1271) — an unauthenticated request is processed even with OAuth installed — *Gap AUTH-6 (2025 lane only); no test asserts a valid Mcp-Session-Id cannot substitute for a bearer* |
| MCP servers MUST use secure, non-deterministic session IDs; generated IDs SHOULD use secure random number generators; avoid predictable/sequential identifiers | MUST | 🟡 partial | 2026 default path issues no wire session ID (stateless; ephemeral internal sessions only) — requirement moot there. 2025 lane: Uuid::now_v7 (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:328,956; crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/mcp_session.rs:47) — 74 random bits from the OS RNG but timestamp-prefixed (deliberate, for temporal ordering). No test asserts randomness/unpredictability — *UUIDv7 is non-sequential in its random portion; the timestamp prefix is disclosed by design — acceptable but worth a documented disposition* |
| MCP servers SHOULD bind session IDs to user-specific information (e.g. <user_id>:<session_id> keying) when storing/transmitting session data | SHOULD | ❌ gap | No user binding anywhere in turul-mcp-session-storage or SessionManager — events/sessions are keyed by session_id alone (e.g. stream_manager.rs, session storage traits). Auth claims are request-scoped and never joined to the session key — *Gap AUTH-7; 2025 lane only (2026 default path has no client-visible sessions)* |
| Local MCP server compromise: an MCP client supporting one-click local server configuration MUST implement consent (show exact command, identify danger, require approval, allow cancel); SHOULD add guardrails/sandboxing | MUST | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client is a library with no installer/one-click configuration surface; it executes no user-configured startup commands |
| MCP servers intended to run locally SHOULD use stdio transport, or restrict HTTP access (auth token, IPC) | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Framework provides the restriction mechanisms: bearer-auth pre-session middleware (turul-mcp-oauth) and bind-address configuration on McpServer::builder(); deployment guidance, not enforceable in-library — *INFO-grade: mechanisms exist; no default forces them for localhost binds* |
| Scope minimization (guidance): servers should emit precise scope challenges, avoid returning the full catalog in every challenge, log elevation events; clients should begin with baseline scopes | INFO | 🟡 partial | Initial 401 challenge always emits the full configured scopes_supported (middleware.rs:40-53) — endorsed for the INITIAL challenge by the Authorization page's Scope Selection Strategy, but no per-operation insufficient_scope challenges exist (see AUTH-2), so progressive elevation is impossible — *Non-normative section; tracked through AUTH-2 rather than its own gap* |

## MCP draft Server Utilities: Caching (client consumption)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/utilities/caching>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Clients **MUST NOT** serve a cached response for a request whose method or parameters differ from the request that produced it"; results produced by retrying via the multi round-trip requests (MRTR) mechanism **MUST NOT** be cached, as they depend on inputs outside the cache key | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client implements no response cache at all — no `ttlMs`/`cacheScope`/`CacheableResult` consumption anywhere in `crates/turul-mcp-client/src` (the 2026 result parser reads both fields off the wire and drops them before returning the version-neutral type, `protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:240`), so both cache-key invariants are vacuously satisfied — *mirrors the disposition of the existing "cacheScope private" row (Schema Reference section, line 756): if client-side caching is ever added, this becomes a live requirement.* |
| `ttlMs` client-consumption semantics: absent ⇒ client **SHOULD** assume default `0` (immediately stale); negative ⇒ client **SHOULD** treat as `0`; once expired the response is stale and client **SHOULD** re-fetch on next access; client **MAY** re-fetch every time, **MAY** re-fetch early on suspected change, **MAY** serve stale responses if re-fetch errors; clients **SHOULD NOT** treat TTL as a polling interval triggering automatic background refetches; implementations that do poll **MUST** apply jitter and backoff | SHOULD | ❌ gap | No `ttlMs` consumption logic exists in `turul-mcp-client` — the 2026 result parser reads and discards both cache fields (`protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:240`, comment: "dropped by the remap"); there is no cache store, no freshness/staleness check, and no polling loop to apply jitter/backoff to. Every clause in this cluster is unaddressed rather than satisfied — client-side caching is unimplemented end-to-end. |
| "Servers **MAY** change the underlying data before the TTL expires." | MAY | ➖ n/a | Server-authored data-freshness freedom, not a framework contract — the framework only carries the `ttlMs` value the server author sets on `CacheableResult` (`crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/caching.rs`); it has no opinion on whether the underlying data changes within that window. |
| cacheScope "public": any client, shared gateway, or caching proxy **MAY** store and serve the cached response to any user; cached responses **MAY** be reused for the same authorization context | MAY | ➖ n/a | Same disposition as the existing "cacheScope private" row (Schema Reference section, line 756): turul-mcp-client ships no response cache or gateway, so the permissive MAY is not exercised either way — the "public" counterpart of that row. |
| A server **MAY** provide `ttlMs` without advertising `listChanged: true`; a server **MAY** advertise both `listChanged: true` **and** provide `ttlMs` | MAY | 🧪 implemented | `listChanged` (per-capability bool, e.g. `ToolsCapabilities.list_changed` in `src/initialize.rs`) and `ttlMs` (`CacheableResult.ttl_ms` in `src/caching.rs`) are independent fields on unrelated types with no coupling code between them, so both combinations are structurally possible — no test constructs a server advertising both simultaneously to prove the combination round-trips on the wire. |
| Servers **MAY** return different `ttlMs` values on different pages; when a cached page expires the client **SHOULD** re-fetch that page using its cursor; clients that require a consistent full-list snapshot **SHOULD** re-fetch from the beginning (without a cursor); if a cursor becomes invalid the client **SHOULD** discard all cached pages and re-fetch from the beginning | SHOULD | ❌ gap | Per-page `ttlMs` variance is possible at the type level (`ttl_ms` is set per-response, e.g. `resources.rs:228-231`), but none of the client-side refetch behaviors exist — `turul-mcp-client` keeps no cache of prior pages to discard, has no cursor-triggered refetch-on-expiry, and has no snapshot-consistency mode (`list_resources`/`list_tools` in `client.rs` each issue one page per call with no cache layer above them). |
| Servers **MUST** apply the same `cacheScope` to all response pages for a given list request | MUST | 🟡 partial | Each list handler serializes `cacheScope` from one fixed default constant per result type (`CacheableResult::default_public()`/`default_private()`, `caching.rs:91,96`, wired into `resources.rs`/`tools.rs`/`prompts.rs`), so cross-page drift is structurally unlikely — but no test fetches two pages of the same list request and asserts identical `cacheScope`; `discover_stateless_2026.rs:185-212` only asserts presence per single response, not cross-page equality. |
| Servers **MUST** be aware that "public" `cacheScope` responses may be shared between callers even from an authenticated endpoint; servers **MUST** apply appropriate per-primitive access controls, and **MUST NOT** rely on `cacheScope` alone to prevent unauthorized access to primitives | MUST | ➖ n/a | `cacheScope` is modeled purely as a caching hint (`caching.rs`); the framework makes no authorization decisions based on it and ships no built-in per-primitive access-control system — access control for resources/tools remains entirely server-author territory (AGENTS.md "Resources Compliance — Security: enforce roots and access controls"). This is deployment guidance for server authors, not a framework-testable contract. |

## Roots (deprecated client feature, rides MRTR input requests)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/client/roots>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Deprecated per SEP-2577: "New implementations SHOULD NOT adopt it; existing implementations SHOULD migrate to passing directories or files via tool parameters, resource URIs, or server configuration" | SHOULD NOT | ✅ compliant | #[deprecated(since="0.4.0")] on Root/ListRootsRequest/ListRootsResult (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/roots.rs:27,83,102) and RootsCapabilities (src/initialize.rs:72-77) — *Markers compliant; the inbound roots/list registration is now 2025-gated (FIXED 2026-06-11, see CF/GAP-CF-4): on 2026 it answers 404 + -32601 along with notifications/roots/list_changed (error_mapping_2026.rs::methods_absent_from_the_2026_schema_get_404, red-phase recorded).* |
| "Clients that support roots MUST declare the roots capability in _meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities on each request" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Client declares via `DeclaredCapabilities.roots` into every request `_meta` (mapping unit-tested); server side gated + tested in `roots_and_sampling_capability_arms_are_gated` |
| Servers retrieve roots by returning an InputRequiredResult containing a roots/list request (method string "roots/list") | INFO | ✅ compliant | InputRequest::ListRoots with method-dispatched Deserialize (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/input_required.rs:55-93); test input_request_list_roots_serializes_with_method_string (input_required.rs:413) |
| Response wire shape { roots: [{ uri, name? }] } | INFO | ✅ compliant | ListRootsResult (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/roots.rs:108-114); test test_list_roots_result_matches_typescript_spec (roots.rs:268) asserts roots-only shape |
| "uri: ... This MUST be a file:// URI in the current specification" | MUST | 🟡 partial | Root::validate() rejects non-file:// (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/roots.rs:67-72); test test_root_validation (roots.rs:204) — *Validation is an opt-in helper; nothing enforces it when an application serializes InputResponse::ListRoots* |
| Error handling: "the client does not need to replay the initial call with an error message as the server is not waiting" (MRTR pattern) | INFO | ✅ compliant | MRTR is request/response — input_required_to_result completes the HTTP exchange (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:55-98); no blocking wait exists anywhere on the 2026 path |
| Clients MUST: only expose roots with appropriate permissions; validate all root URIs against path traversal; implement access controls; monitor accessibility | MUST | ➖ n/a | The client library never auto-answers roots/list; the host application constructs InputResponse::ListRoots itself (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/error.rs:42-49 surfaces raw input_requests). Permission/traversal policy is host-app territory; only Root::validate() is shipped as a helper |
| Servers SHOULD: handle roots becoming unavailable; respect root boundaries; validate paths against provided roots | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Framework does not consume roots for filesystem operations; boundary enforcement is server-author business logic |
| Clients SHOULD prompt users for consent before exposing roots and provide root-management UI | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Headless client library — UI/consent belongs to the embedding application |
| "Servers SHOULD check for roots capability before usage" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | -32021 gate covers InputRequest::ListRoots (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:71-73) — *Only the elicitation arm of the gate is wire-tested (undeclared_capability_is_rejected_with_32021, crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs:191); roots arm untested — GAP-CF-6* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs:919-933 now includes test `roots_and_sampling_capability_arms_are_gated()` that validates the roots arm of the -32021 capability gate. The test verifies both undeclared (→ -32021) and declared (→ input_required) paths. Commit 3ad11118 ('feat: server wire-edges P2 batch') added this wire test, closing gap CF-6. Error code corrected -32003 → -32021 per the 2026-07 renumbering (audit §6.1); the RE-GRADED note's original -32003 citations were accurate as of 2026-06-12 but the code has since moved to -32021.

## Sampling (deprecated client feature, rides MRTR input requests)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/client/sampling>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Deprecated per SEP-2577: "New implementations SHOULD NOT adopt it; existing implementations SHOULD migrate to integrating directly with LLM provider APIs" | SHOULD NOT | ✅ compliant | #[deprecated(since="0.4.0")] on all sampling types (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:58,85,124,183,260,279,302,348,368); inbound sampling/createMessage handler is cfg-gated to the 2025-11-25 lane (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:159-163) |
| "Clients that support sampling MUST declare the sampling capability in _meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities on each request" | MUST | 🧪 implemented | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:27-29 emits sampling:{} when DeclaredCapabilities.sampling=true (src/config.rs:46-47); no wire test asserts the sampling declaration | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:34-40 implements sampling capability emission in request_meta() based on DeclaredCapabilities.sampling (line 34) — when true, it constructs ClientCapabilities with the sampling field populated. However, config.rs:53 shows sampling defaults to false, so the baseline client never declares sampling unless explicitly enabled. Implemented but untested
| "Clients MUST declare support for tool use via the sampling.tools capability to receive tool-enabled sampling requests" | MUST | ✅ compliant | DeclaredCapabilities is boolean-only (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/config.rs:43-50) — sampling.tools can never be declared, so the client never falsely claims it — *Safe-by-omission, but tool-enabled sampling is unreachable for bilingual-client applications — GAP-CF-5* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): config.rs:42-63 defines DeclaredCapabilities.sampling_tools as a boolean field (line 57). v2026_07_28.rs:36 emits sampling.tools capability when declared.sampling_tools is true: `tools: declared.sampling_tools.then(Default::default)`. The requirement states clients cannot declare sampling.tools without sampling being true; this is enforced at the config level since sampling_tools must be explicitl
| "Servers MUST NOT send tool-enabled sampling requests to Clients that have not declared support ... via the sampling.tools capability" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (8fd899cf, GAP-CF-2): `input_required_to_result` requires `sampling.tools` when the CreateMessage params carry tools/toolChoice (→ -32021, HTTP 400; code was -32003 at fix time, renumbered since — audit §6.1). Wire test `mrtr_2026.rs::tool_enabled_sampling_requires_the_tools_subcapability` (red phase recorded) |
| includeContext "thisServer"/"allServers" deprecated (SEP-2596): servers SHOULD avoid them and SHOULD NOT use them unless the client declares sampling.context | SHOULD NOT | 🟡 partial | Documented (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:12-14,299-301; src/initialize.rs:107-111); include_context is an open Option<String> and no gate inspects it against caps.sampling.context |
| sampling/createMessage is delivered via InputRequiredResult (no standalone server-initiated request) | INFO | ✅ compliant | InputRequest::CreateMessage with explicit method-string dispatch (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/input_required.rs:55-93); InputRequiredResult invariant tests (input_required.rs:317-445) |
| "When a user message contains tool results it MUST contain ONLY tool results" and "every assistant message containing ToolUseContent blocks MUST be followed by a user message consisting entirely of ToolResultContent blocks with matching toolUseId" (also Security #6) | MUST | ❌ gap | SamplingMessageContent permits arbitrary mixed Vec<SamplingMessageContentBlock> (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:269-274); no validation helper or rejection path exists anywhere in the workspace — GAP-CF-9 |
| "Sampling messages MUST contain a role field of 'user' or 'assistant'" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Role enum has only User/Assistant (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:54-56 re-export of prompts::Role); serde rejects any other value at deserialize |
| "The list of messages in a sampling request SHOULD NOT be retained between separate requests" | SHOULD NOT | ➖ n/a | Stateless MRTR; neither client nor server crate stores sampling messages (no retention surface exists in crates/turul-mcp-client/src or turul-mcp-server/src) |
| toolChoice modes: auto (default, absent), required, none | INFO | ✅ compliant | ToolChoiceMode + effective_mode() absent-means-auto (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:111-173); tests test_tool_choice_absent_mode_is_auto, test_tool_choice_mode_serializes_as_required (sampling.rs:714-747) |
| "maxTokens: Maximum tokens to generate; required" and "The client MUST respect the maxTokens parameter" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Required at type level (max_tokens: u32, crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:326-327) — *Client-side honoring is n/a — the library runs no LLM; the embedding app receives the raw request* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): sampling.rs:326-327: max_tokens is required at type level (pub max_tokens: u32, non-optional). CreateMessageRequestParams serializes this field unconditionally. The requirement is satisfied at the protocol layer. The row's comment states 'client-side honoring is n/a — the library runs no LLM' which is accurate: the Rust client library receives and passes the request to the embedding application; t
| Client MAY modify/ignore systemPrompt, includeContext, temperature, stopSequences, metadata; MAY map model hints to other providers | MAY | ➖ n/a | Headless client library performs no model selection; ModelPreferences/ModelHint bound for transport only (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:64-108) |
| stopReason is non-exhaustive: "implementations MAY provide their own arbitrary values" | MAY | ✅ compliant | stop_reason: Option<String> open string (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs:381-382) |
| "There SHOULD always be a human in the loop with the ability to deny sampling requests"; clients SHOULD implement user approval controls and rate limiting | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | McpClientError::InputRequired surfaces the request to the host app for its approval flow (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/error.rs:42-49); UI/rate-limit policy is app territory |
| Applications **SHOULD**: provide UI that makes it easy and intuitive to review sampling requests; allow users to view and edit prompts before sending; present generated responses for review before delivery | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Same disposition as the existing row above (line 495, "human in the loop ... user approval controls"): `McpClientError::InputRequired` surfaces the raw `CreateMessage` request to the host application (`crates/turul-mcp-client/src/error.rs:42-49`); review/edit/approve UI is host-app territory in this headless library. |
| Implementations wrapping providers that support disabling parallel tool use **MAY** expose this as an extension; not part of core MCP | MAY | ➖ n/a | No such extension is shipped — `crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/sampling.rs` models no parallel-tool-use field, and the framework has no LLM-provider-wrapping role to attach an extension to (sampling requests are only relayed via MRTR, never executed in-framework). |
| Both parties **SHOULD** validate message content | SHOULD | ❌ gap | `SamplingMessageContent` permits arbitrary mixed `Vec<SamplingMessageContentBlock>` with no validation helper anywhere in the workspace (`sampling.rs:269-274`) — same underlying defect as the existing GAP-CF-9 row ("user message with tool results MUST contain ONLY tool results"), now graded against the broader "validate message content" security-consideration wording. |
| Clients **SHOULD** respect model preference hints | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Same disposition as the existing row (line 493, "Client MAY modify/ignore ... MAY map model hints to other providers"): the headless client performs no model selection at all — `ModelPreferences`/`ModelHint` are bound for transport only (`sampling.rs:64-108`) and handed to the embedding application, which owns whether/how hints are honored. |
| "Both parties MUST handle sensitive data appropriately" | MUST | ➖ n/a | Deployment-level requirement; not expressible as framework code |
| "Both parties SHOULD implement iteration limits for tool loops" | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | The multi-turn loop lives in server-author tool code; framework neither drives nor caps it |

## Elicitation (form + URL modes, rides MRTR input requests)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/client/elicitation>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Servers MUST NOT use form mode elicitation to request sensitive information ... MUST use URL mode for interactions involving such sensitive information" | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | Content decision of the server author; framework ships both modes (ElicitRequest::new_form/new_url, crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:388-416) |
| Clients MUST: show which server is requesting; clear decline/cancel; review-before-send (form); display target domain + gather consent (URL) | MUST | ➖ n/a | Headless library — raw input_requests are surfaced via McpClientError::InputRequired (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/error.rs:42-49) for the host app's UI |
| "Clients that support elicitation MUST declare the elicitation capability in _meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities on each request" | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/config.rs:44-45 + src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs:24-26; e2e mrtr_round_trip_through_the_bilingual_client (crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs:149) proves it end-to-end — the real server's -32021 gate rejects without the declaration |
| "For backwards compatibility, an empty capabilities object is equivalent to declaring support for form mode only" | INFO | 🟡 partial | Documented (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/initialize.rs:130-131) — *Server gate never evaluates modes, so the equivalence has no enforcing consumer — see the mode-gating row* |
| "Clients declaring the elicitation capability MUST support at least one mode (form or url)" | MUST | 🟡 partial | Declaration is opt-in default-off with doc 'declare only what the application actually handles' (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/config.rs:35-38); library cannot verify the app's handler exists |
| "Servers MUST NOT send elicitation requests with modes that are not supported by the client" | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (8fd899cf, GAP-CF-1): mode-aware gate — URL-mode elicitation requires `elicitation.url`; empty `elicitation: {}` declares form-only; url-only does not imply form (→ -32021, HTTP 400; code was -32003 at fix time, renumbered since — audit §6.1). Wire tests `mrtr_2026.rs::url_mode_elicitation_requires_the_url_subcapability` / `::form_mode_elicitation_passes_with_empty_capability_object` |
| "Servers MAY request information ... by sending an InputRequiredResult containing an elicitation/create request" | MAY | ✅ compliant | McpError::InputRequired → input_required_to_result on tools/call, resources/read, prompts/get (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:55-98, src/server.rs:2150-2160); real-HTTP e2e mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call + mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read + mrtr_round_trip_on_prompts_get (crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs:143,382,422) |
| "All elicitation requests MUST include ... mode (optional for form) and message" | MUST | ✅ compliant | message required at type level in both params variants (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:316-347); tests form_mode_round_trips_with_optional_mode / url_mode_round_trips_with_required_mode_and_fields (tests/compliance.rs:831,841) |
| "Form mode elicitation requests MUST either specify mode:'form' or omit the mode field, and include requestedSchema" | MUST | ✅ compliant | ElicitRequestFormParams with required requested_schema (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:316-328); test test_elicit_request_matches_typescript_spec (elicitation.rs:1025) |
| requestedSchema restricted to flat objects with primitive properties: string/number/boolean/enum (single+multi-select, titled via oneOf/anyOf const+title pairs, legacy enumNames) | INFO | ✅ compliant | PrimitiveSchemaDefinition/EnumSchema unions with deny_unknown_fields preventing silent collapse (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:88-286); enum_union_fidelity_tests (elicitation.rs:1119-1254) + elicitation_enum_schemas (tests/compliance.rs:901+) |
| "Clients MUST treat requests without a mode field as form mode" | MUST | ✅ compliant | Untagged ElicitRequestParams union, URL tried first by unique fields (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:369-376); test untagged_discrimination_picks_form_when_mode_absent (tests/compliance.rs:873) |
| "Clients that support defaults SHOULD pre-populate form fields with these values" | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | UI concern; default fields are modeled on all schema types (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:21,41,59,113) |
| "URL mode elicitation requests MUST specify mode:'url', a message, url and elicitationId; the url parameter MUST contain a valid URL" | MUST | 🟡 partial | All four fields required at type level (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:332-347); tests at tests/compliance.rs:841-869 — *No syntactic URL validation is performed on the url field* |
| "Servers MAY send notifications/elicitation/complete ... MUST only send to the client that initiated ... MUST include the elicitationId" | MAY | 🟡 partial | Binding exists with method string and required elicitationId (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/notifications.rs:441-470, unit test at :803) — *No framework emission path routes it on the 2026 lane (no hits in turul-mcp-server/src or turul-http-mcp-server/src); untested through subscriptions/listen — GAP-CF-7* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): notifications.rs:441-470 defines ElicitationCompleteNotification with required elicitationId (line 451: pub elicitation_id: String). Unit test at line 803 verifies the shape. The mechanism exists but is untested through subscript
| "Clients MUST ignore notifications referencing unknown or already-completed IDs; MAY wait for it to auto-retry; SHOULD still provide manual controls" | MUST | 🧪 untested | Client forwards notifications raw to the application; no elicitationId tracking, dedup, or auto-retry exists in crates/turul-mcp-client/src — the MUST is silently delegated without guidance — GAP-CF-7 |
| Three-action response model: accept (content for form, omitted for URL) / decline / cancel | INFO | ✅ compliant | ElicitAction + ElicitResult with optional content (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:510-554); tests test_elicit_result, test_elicit_result_matches_typescript_spec (elicitation.rs:966,1060) |
| "If state is stored, servers ... MUST securely associate this state with individual users"; user identity from MCP authorization | MUST | 🟡 partial | MRTR path is stateless by design; requestState round-trips opaque and SessionContext::mrtr_request_state docs warn it is attacker-controlled, advising HMAC before authorization use (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:349-355) — *Integrity/binding implementation left entirely to the server author; no helper shipped* |
| "Servers SHOULD NOT assume elicitation requests will always succeed, and MUST handle cases where the user declines or cancels ... or the client fails" | MUST | 🟡 partial | Tri-state ElicitAction is surfaced to the tool via SessionContext::input_responses (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:339-348) — *Only the accept path is exercised in e2e (mrtr_2026.rs); decline/cancel legs untested* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): session.rs:339-348 exposes input_responses() and input_responses_decline_cancel_leg_untested. ElicitAction is tri-state (accept/decline/cancel). mrtr_2026.rs tests the accept path (gated_echo_tool line 32-50 demonstrates accept flow). No e2e test exercises the decline or cancel legs of ElicitAction — the trio-state surface is available but two-thirds untested through acceptance tests. Suggested gr
| Servers MUST bind elicitation requests to client+user identity; MUST verify the identity of the user who opens the URL (phishing); MUST NOT rely on client-provided user identification without verification | MUST | ➖ n/a | Deployment/auth-layer responsibility; no URL-elicitation server flow is shipped in-framework. Identity primitives live in crates/turul-mcp-oauth |
