| URL mode elicitation for OAuth flows: servers **MUST NOT** rely on URL mode elicitation to authorize users for themselves; third-party credentials **MUST NOT** transit through the MCP client; the server **MUST NOT** reuse the client's own credentials for a third-party service (token passthrough, forbidden); the user **MUST** authorize the MCP server directly, outside the MCP protocol; the server **MUST NOT** transmit credentials obtained via URL mode elicitation to the client | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | Content/flow decision entirely owned by the server author — the framework ships the URL-mode elicitation wire type (`ElicitRequest::new_url`, `crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/elicitation.rs:452`) as an opaque `url`/`message` carrier and has no OAuth-flow-specific code path at all; mirrors the disposition of the neighboring row above (line 523) — no URL-elicitation server flow is shipped in-framework. |
| Clients **SHOULD** implement user approval controls; **SHOULD** allow users to decline elicitation requests at any time; **SHOULD** present elicitation requests in a way that makes clear what information is requested and why | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Headless library — raw `input_requests` (including elicitation) are surfaced via `McpClientError::InputRequired` (`crates/turul-mcp-client/src/error.rs:42-49`) for the host application's UI to implement approval, decline, and presentation; same disposition as the existing row (line 506, "show which server is requesting"). The `Decline` action is itself modeled and unconditionally acceptable at the protocol level (`ElicitAction::Decline`, `elicitation.rs:556`, round-trip test `elicitation.rs:1090-1099`) — the "at any time" UI affordance remains host-app territory. |
| Safe URL handling sub-bullets: **SHOULD NOT** render URLs as clickable in any elicitation field except the URL-mode `url` field; **SHOULD** highlight the domain to mitigate subdomain spoofing; **SHOULD** warn on ambiguous/Punycode URIs | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Folds under the existing "Safe URL handling" row (line 524, same page/section) — same disposition: server-author content rules and host-app UI rendering rules; the framework transports the `url` field verbatim and renders nothing. |
| Identifying the user: servers **SHOULD** follow security best practices; to prevent phishing the server **MUST** ensure the user who started the elicitation is the same user who completes the authorization flow, and **MUST** ensure the identity mechanism is resilient to an attacker modifying the elicitation URL | MUST | ➖ n/a | Same disposition as the existing row (line 523, "Servers MUST bind elicitation requests to client+user identity ... verify the identity of the user who opens the URL"): no URL-elicitation server flow ships in-framework; identity primitives (JWT `sub` claim, `crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/middleware.rs:141`) are available to a server author who builds one, but the started-vs-completed-user binding and anti-tampering mechanism are not implemented by the framework. |
| State storage **MUST** be protected against unauthorized access; for remote MCP servers, user identification **MUST** be derived from credentials acquired via MCP authorization when possible (e.g. the `sub` claim) | MUST | ➖ n/a | Distinct facet of the existing row (line 521, "MUST securely associate this state with individual users"): the framework implements no elicitation state store at all (MRTR is stateless; `requestState` round-trips opaquely, `crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:349-355`), so there is no storage for the framework to protect. `claims.sub` is already extracted and available per-request (`crates/turul-mcp-oauth/src/middleware.rs:141`) for a server author to use, but the framework does not itself derive elicitation-user-identity from it. |
| Safe URL handling: servers MUST NOT include sensitive end-user info or pre-authenticated URLs; SHOULD use HTTPS; clients MUST NOT pre-fetch or auto-open, MUST show the full URL, MUST open securely without client/LLM inspection | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | Server-author content rules and host-app UI rules; framework transports the url field verbatim without fetching or opening it |
| Form security: "Clients SHOULD validate all responses against the provided schema" and "Servers SHOULD validate received data matches the requested schema" | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | No validator exists: server hands raw InputResponse content to the tool unvalidated (crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:344-348); client performs no schema validation of app-supplied responses — GAP-CF-8 — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12 (CF/GAP-CF-8 fixed as a helper)**: `turul_mcp_builders::validate_elicit_content(schema, content)` validates the primitive form surface (required/unknown keys, types, bounds, enum membership); central framework enforcement is impossible-by-design on the stateless lane (the leg-1 schema is not retained server-side), so tools call it on the retry — demonstrated in `mrtr-elicitation-server`. Client-side validation remains app-level. |

## Server Features Overview (informational page — no MUST/SHOULD requirements)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Servers provide Prompts (user-controlled templates) via prompts/list & prompts/get | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:1081 (with_prompts); test: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::prompts_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result — *Page is a non-normative overview of the three primitives + control hierarchy; detailed prompt requirements live on /server/prompts (separate section).* |
| Servers provide Resources (application-controlled contextual data) via resources/list & resources/read | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:1097-1153 (with_resources); test: discover_stateless_2026.rs::resources_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result — *Detailed resource requirements live on /server/resources (separate section).* |
| Servers provide Tools (model-controlled executable functions) via tools/list & tools/call | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:483-497 (ListToolsHandler + tool_handler registration); tests: discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_call_dispatches_without_session_handshake, ::tools_list_advertises_output_schema — *Detailed tool requirements live on /server/tools (separate section).* |

## server/discover (Discovery)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/discover>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "server/discover lets a client query a server's supported protocol versions, capabilities, and identity... Servers MUST implement it." | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:504-514 (DiscoverHandler registered under protocol-2026-07-28), :1405-1469 (handler impl); test: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::server_discover_answers_without_a_session (real-HTTP, asserts 200 + wire shape, no session) — *Stateless: handler reads only static implementation/capabilities/instructions — creates no session, per ADR-027 stateless core.* |
| Request shape: DiscoverRequest { method: "server/discover", params: RequestParams } — "carries no body parameters beyond the standard _meta" | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/discover.rs:32-57; tests: discover.rs::discover_request_method_is_server_discover, ::discover_request_has_params_object; tests/compliance.rs::server_discover_binding (line 1816) + ::server_discover_method_is_present_positive_control (line 2046) — *Server also enforces the stateless-core required _meta (protocolVersion/clientInfo/clientCapabilities) on discover like every 2026 request — streamable_http.rs:1598-1670; tests discover_stateless_2026.rs::missing_meta_is_rejected_with_invalid_params, ::incomplete_meta_missing_client_capabilities_is_rejected.* |
| Response field supportedVersions: string[] — required. "Protocol versions the server supports." | MUST | ✅ compliant | discover.rs:81 (required field, no Option); wire test: discover_stateless_2026.rs:110-113 asserts supportedVersions[0]=="2026-07-28"; unit: discover.rs::discover_result_serializes_required_fields |
| Response field capabilities: ServerCapabilities — required | MUST | ✅ compliant | discover.rs:84; wire test: discover_stateless_2026.rs:114 asserts capabilities is_object; handler passes builder's auto-configured capabilities (server.rs:508-512) |
| Response field serverInfo: Implementation — required | MUST | ✅ compliant | discover.rs:87; wire test: discover_stateless_2026.rs:115 asserts serverInfo.name=="discover-2026-test" |
| Response field instructions: string — optional. "Natural-language guidance for LLMs on how to use this server effectively." | MAY | 🟡 partial | Implemented: builder.rs:335-337 (.instructions()) → server.rs:1459-1461 (DiscoverHandler attaches it). Unit tests only: discover.rs::discover_result_serializes_instructions_when_present, ::discover_result_omits_optional_fields_when_none. No wire-level test builds a server with .instructions() and asserts it appears in the server/discover response (grep over crates/*/tests/*.rs: zero hits). — *Production path (Builder → DiscoverHandler → HTTP) untested — CLAUDE.md §Test Coverage Discipline #2.* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Builder method and framework plumbing implemented (builder.rs:356-358, server.rs:1472-1474). CLAUDE.md §Test Coverage Discipline #2 requires production-path wire test: no test in discover_stateless_2026.rs or wire_edges_2026.rs asserts instructions field on server/discover response. Suggest 🧪 — add wire test.
| "This operation supports caching" — DiscoverResult extends CacheableResult: ttlMs (required, "If 0, the response SHOULD be considered immediately stale") and cacheScope: "public"\|"private" (required) on the wire | MUST | 🟡 partial | discover.rs:73-76 (required ttl_ms/cache_scope), :109-110 (defaults ttlMs=0, cacheScope=public — spec-valid immediately-stale); unit test discover.rs::discover_result_round_trips asserts ttlMs==0 && cacheScope=="public". Gap: discover_stateless_2026.rs never asserts ttlMs/cacheScope on the discover wire response (it does for resources/list:195 and prompts/list:211), and DiscoverHandler::new (server.rs:1421-1433) offers no way to set a non-zero TTL — DiscoverResult::with_cache (discover.rs:120-124) is never called from the framework. — *ttlMs=0/public is conservative-compliant; the missing knob means a deployment can never advertise a fresh discover cache (the spec example shows ttlMs: 3600000). ADR-027 Phase 7 tracks cache-field finalization.* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): ttl_ms/cache_scope fields implemented (discover.rs:73-76, 109-110, 120-124). wire_edges_2026.rs:325 asserts ttlMs on tools/list. BUT: (1) no test asserts ttlMs on discover response (discover_stateless_2026.rs:78-116 never checks it), (2) DiscoverHandler::new (server.rs:1421-1433) offers no way to set non-zero TTL for custom deployments. Test assertion gap + zero-TTL hardcoding. Suggest 🟡.
| Result discriminator resultType: "complete" (CacheableResult extends Result; absent resultType is treated as complete) | MUST | ✅ compliant | discover.rs:69-70 (#[serde(default)] ResultType::Complete); wire test: discover_stateless_2026.rs:109 asserts result.resultType=="complete"; back-compat: discover.rs::discover_result_back_compat_accepts_missing_result_type |
| Capability truthfulness — resources.subscribe advertised only when per-URI resource updates are actually served (schema: "Whether this server supports subscribing to resource updates") | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | builder.rs:1101-1111 & 1622-1634 (2026: subscribe=true, backed by transport subscriptions/listen; 2025: false); tests: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::resources_subscribe_capability_is_advertised_truthfully (asserts via server/discover) + ::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types (proves delivery of watched notifications/resources/updated) — *Repo policy AGENTS.md: "Always advertise only the capabilities actually wired." Both halves (advertisement + actual delivery) tested; 2/2 pass.* |
| Capability truthfulness — completions advertised only when completion support is wired | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | builder.rs:1074-1078 (with_completion sets capability + registers handler), :1642-1645 (auto-detect); test: discover_stateless_2026.rs::completion_complete_dispatches_statelessly (asserts capabilities.completions in discover AND completion/complete answers with CompleteResult shape) |
| Capability truthfulness — logging advertised only when the server can actually send notifications/message (schema: "Present if the server supports sending log messages"; deprecated per SEP-2577) | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | builder.rs:1647-1652: build() sets capabilities.logging = Some(default) UNCONDITIONALLY, regardless of whether any log emitter exists. The delivery mechanism IS wired on 2026 (per-request logLevel gating; tests/log_gating_2026.rs::message_notifications_require_a_declared_log_level + ::declared_level_is_the_severity_threshold, 4/4 pass), so the advertisement is mechanically backed — but no test asserts the discover-advertised logging capability, and servers with no log-emitting handlers still advertise it. — *Tension with AGENTS.md capability-truthfulness rule; defensible (SessionContext::notify_log is always available to any tool) but undocumented and untested at the discover boundary.* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): wire_edges_2026.rs::discover_declares_the_logging_capability (line 471) asserts logging capability appears in discover response (line 481). Per-request logLevel gating tested separately in tests/log_gating_2026.rs. ✅ wire test covers the discover-level capability advertisement requirement.
| "Calling server/discover is optional for clients — a client may invoke any RPC inline and handle UnsupportedProtocolVersionError" | MAY | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:277-284 (explicit version hint skips the probe = inline path), :286-300 (bilingual probe), :373-383 (-32022/JSON-RPC error codes surfaced to classify_probe); tests: crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_negotiation.rs::bilingual_client_locks_2026_when_server_answers_discover, ::bilingual_client_locks_2025_when_server_lacks_discover, ::bilingual_client_aborts_on_4xx_without_downgrade; real-server: tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server |
| "A client that supports both modern (per-request _meta) and legacy (initialize handshake) servers SHOULD send server/discover first" (stdio backward-compatibility probe) | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client ships only HTTP transports — crates/turul-mcp-client/src/transport/ contains http.rs and sse.rs; no stdio transport exists, so the stdio-specific probe ordering rule has no target. (On HTTP the bilingual client does probe discover first anyway: client.rs:286.) |
| supportedVersions: "The client should choose a version from this list for use in subsequent requests" | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (DISC-1): the discover body is parsed into `DiscoveredServer` (supportedVersions/instructions/capabilities retained, accessors exposed); -32022 negotiation honors `data.supported` (fallback only on a mutual version). E2e `progress_feed_and_discovered_server_accessors` |
| 2025-11-25 servers answering server/discover: bilingual probe distinguishes -32601 Method Not Found (legacy server → fall back) from other errors (understood discover → never downgrade) | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1546-1554 (2025 lane returns method_not_found for server/discover, enabling the probe); crates/turul-mcp-client/src/version.rs:71-115 (classify_probe rules) with unit tests version.rs::discover_ok_locks_2026 etc. — *Load-bearing for the ADR-030 bilingual contract and the ADR-027 amendment (d) publication gate.* |

## Server Features: Prompts

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/prompts>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Servers that support prompts MUST declare the `prompts` capability (with `listChanged`) in their `DiscoverResult` | MUST | 🟡 partial | Implemented: builder.rs:1609-1614 auto-sets PromptsCapabilities{list_changed:Some(false)} when prompts registered; with_prompts() builder.rs:1081-1089; served by DiscoverHandler server.rs:1454-1458. No test asserts capabilities.prompts in a server/discover response — discover_stateless_2026.rs:114 only asserts capabilities is an object (completions is the only key asserted, :411) — *Negative leg covered indirectly: subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_ack_omits_unsupported_types relies on caps.prompts being absent on a no-prompts server* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Implementation confirmed: builder.rs:1609-1614 auto-sets PromptsCapabilities{list_changed:Some(false)}; server.rs:1454-1458 DiscoverHandler serves it. CLAUDE.md §Test Coverage Discipline #2: no production-path wire test asserts capabilities.prompts appears in server/discover response. discover_stateless_2026.rs:114 only asserts capabilities is an object. Suggest 🧪.
| Servers that declare the prompts capability MUST respond to `prompts/list`; the set MAY be empty | MUST | ✅ compliant | Default PromptsListHandler/PromptsGetHandler registered unconditionally at builder.rs:148-155 (empty set responds, not method-not-found); test crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::prompts_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result (real-HTTP 2026 path, server has with_prompts() with zero prompts → 200 + prompts array) |
| The prompt set MUST NOT vary per-connection or as a side effect of other requests on the connection | MUST NOT | 🟡 partial | Structurally satisfied: PromptsListHandler reads only a build-time immutable HashMap (handlers/mod.rs:230-231, 291-305); no session/connection input reaches the listing. No test asserts cross-request/cross-connection invariance on the 2026 path — *Static registry makes variation impossible; stable name-sorted ordering tested on the 2025 lane (tests/prompts/tests/prompts_endpoints_integration.rs::test_stable_prompt_ordering)* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Structural invariant holds by construction: PromptsListHandler:290-305 reads immutable build-time HashMap; no session/connection input. However, CLAUDE.md §Test Coverage Discipline #4 revert-and-fail rule: no test on 2026 path asserts cross-request/cross-connection invariance. Tests exist on 2025-pinned lane (tests/prompts/tests/prompts_endpoints_integration.rs::test_stable_prompt_ordering). Sugge
| The set MAY vary by the authorization presented on the request (scope-filtered listing) | MAY | ➖ n/a | Framework ships no per-authorization prompt filtering (handlers/mod.rs:291 lists all registered prompts); MAY-permission not exercised — static identical set for all callers is a compliant choice |
| `prompts/list` supports pagination (cursor in params, nextCursor in result) | INFO | 🟡 partial | Cursor pagination with stable name ordering implemented handlers/mod.rs:262-348 (limit clamp 1..=1000, reject 0); wire pagination test only on the 2025-pinned lane (tests/prompts/tests/prompts_endpoints_integration.rs::test_prompts_list_pagination_integration); the 2026 wire test (discover_stateless_2026.rs:205) never passes a cursor — *Same handler code serves both feature gates; 2026-path cursor round-trip is uncovered at the wire* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Handler code implements cursor pagination (handlers/mod.rs:262-348). discover_stateless_2026.rs:205 calls prompts/list but never passes cursor parameter. Same code path serves both lanes; pagination is 2025-pinned wire-tested but not 2026-path wire-tested. Suggest 🟡 — code works, 2026-path cursor round-trip uncovered.
| `ListPromptsResult` extends PaginatedResult + CacheableResult (`resultType`, `ttlMs`, `cacheScope` required) | INFO | ✅ compliant | prompts.rs:198-223 (required ttl_ms/cache_scope, resultType default Complete); wire test discover_stateless_2026.rs::prompts_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result asserts resultType+cacheScope; type tests crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/tests/compliance.rs::list_prompts_result_with_cache, ::list_prompts_result_emits_result_type |
| `prompts/get` returns GetPromptResult (description?, messages[]) extending plain Result (no cache fields) | INFO | ✅ compliant | GetPromptResult prompts.rs:398-439 matches schema draft-schema.ts `GetPromptResult extends Result`; handler handlers/mod.rs:404-505; wire test mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_on_prompts_get leg 2 asserts messages[0].content.text; compliance.rs::get_prompt_result_emits_result_type |
| `GetPromptRequestParams extends InputResponseRequestParams` — name, arguments?: {[k]:string}, inputResponses?, requestState? | INFO | ✅ compliant | prompts.rs:261-281 (string-map arguments, MRTR mixin fields, required _meta); compliance.rs::get_prompt_params_arguments_is_string_map, ::get_prompt_params_input_responses_mixin_serializes, ::get_prompt_params_omits_mixin_fields_when_absent |
| Prompt arguments MAY be auto-completed through the completion API (`PromptReference` with type "ref/prompt", name, title) | MAY | 🟡 partial | PromptReference with name+title completion.rs:23-31; type test compliance.rs::prompt_reference_type_field; wire test discover_stateless_2026.rs (completion/complete with ref/prompt returns CompleteResult, :423-437). BUT default CompletionHandler ignores the reference entirely and returns canned values (handlers/mod.rs:208-222) — no linkage from ref/prompt to a registered prompt's argument values unless the user registers a custom completion handler |
| Servers MAY respond to `prompts/get` with an `InputRequiredResult` (MRTR, SEP-2322) | MAY | ✅ compliant | Server: McpError::InputRequired from McpPrompt::render converted via input_required_to_result (handlers/mod.rs:480-491, 55-97 incl. -32021 capability gate); retry leg forwards inputResponses/requestState to render under reserved io.modelcontextprotocol/* keys (handlers/mod.rs:457-475, documented prompt.rs:29-36). Real-HTTP test crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_on_prompts_get (both legs, requestState echo asserted) |
| When retrying, clients include `inputResponses` and, if provided, `requestState` in the request parameters | INFO | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11: `check_result_type` in `v2026_07_28.rs` (unknown resultType → ProtocolError::InvalidResponse; input_required → McpClientError::InputRequired) applied across all 2026 result parsers; retry APIs `read_resource_with_input_responses` / `get_prompt_with_input_responses`; real-server e2e `mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read_through_the_client` / `..._on_prompts_get_...` (revert-and-fail proven) + 2 module unit tests |
| Servers that declared `listChanged` SHOULD send `notifications/prompts/list_changed` to clients with an open subscriptions/listen stream with `promptsListChanged: true` | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | The static framework never declares prompts.listChanged=true (builder.rs:1609-1614 hardcodes Some(false): no dynamic prompt change sources exist), so the SHOULD never binds. Transport delivery plumbing for the method exists and is filter-tested: streamable_http.rs:1937-1938, 2000; negative-filter tests subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types and e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_subscriptions_listen_receives_filtered_notifications (prompts/list_changed broadcast is suppressed when not requested) — *Truthfulness wrinkle: listen honors promptsListChanged on listChanged:false servers — see gap PR-2026-03* |
| Notification wire form: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/prompts/list_changed"} (underscore method string) | INFO | ✅ compliant | Method string constant in subscription gating streamable_http.rs:1938/2000 and protocol method-string table compliance.rs:1757; e2e_2026_real_server.rs EmitOnceTool broadcasts it as a wire-complete JsonRpcNotification (:423-426) |
| Prompt data type: name, title?, description?, icons?, arguments?, _meta? (extends BaseMetadata, Icons) | INFO | ✅ compliant | Protocol type complete: prompts.rs:37-57; compliance.rs::prompt_shape_matches_schema. Server defect: PromptsListHandler rebuilds descriptors with only name/description/arguments (handlers/mod.rs:291-305), silently dropping title, icons, and _meta that PromptDefinition::to_prompt() provides (turul-mcp-builders/src/traits/prompt_traits.rs:205-214) — see gap PR-2026-01 | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Wire test wire_edges_2026.rs::prompt_descriptors_and_error_codes (line 401) asserts on the 2026 path that title (line 414), icons (line 418), and _meta (line 422) survive in prompts/list response. Handler code handlers/mod.rs:427-431 includes with_icons and with_meta. Audit gap PR-2026-01 was fixed in commit 3ad11118 (2026-06-11). ✅
| PromptArgument: name, title?, description?, required? (extends BaseMetadata) | INFO | ✅ compliant | prompts.rs:108-122; compliance.rs::prompt_argument_shape_matches_schema; surfaced in prompts/list handlers/mod.rs:300-302; 2025-lane e2e tests/prompts/tests/mcp_prompts_specification.rs::test_prompt_arguments_mcp_schema_compliance |
| PromptMessage role: either "user" or "assistant" only | INFO | ✅ compliant | Role enum has only User/Assistant (prompts.rs:98-105); compliance.rs::prompt_message_only_user_and_assistant_roles, ::prompt_message_role_values_match_schema |
| Content types text/image/audio/resource_link/resource — all supporting optional annotations | INFO | ✅ compliant | ContentBlock variants each carry annotations + _meta (content.rs:99-153, ResourceLink flattens full Resource incl. size/icons per ADR-027 A4); 2025-lane test tests/prompts/tests/mcp_prompts_specification.rs::test_prompt_content_block_mcp_compliance; protocol content tests in compliance.rs |
| Image/audio data MUST be base64-encoded and include a valid MIME type | MUST | 🟡 partial | Types carry data+mimeType (content.rs:110-134) but constructors accept arbitrary strings — no base64 or MIME validation anywhere on the prompts/get emit path (handlers/mod.rs:495-505 serializes whatever render() returned). No validating test — *Spec burden falls on content producers (user render impls); framework neither validates nor documents the obligation at the trait* |
| Embedded resources MUST include a valid resource URI, MIME type, and text or base64 blob | MUST | 🟡 partial | ResourceContents models TextResourceContents/BlobResourceContents shape (content.rs, re-exported prompts.rs:364); shape tested in compliance.rs content section. URI validity/MIME presence not enforced at emit time (mimeType is Option on ResourceContents per schema; no URI validation) — *Type-level conformance only; same producer-burden caveat as image/audio* |
| Servers SHOULD return -32602 for invalid prompt name, -32602 for missing required arguments, -32603 for internal errors | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Mapping correct: unknown prompt → McpError::invalid_param_type → JsonRpcErrorObject::invalid_params (-32602) (handlers/mod.rs:425-427 + lib.rs:443-450; PromptNotFound also -32602 lib.rs:469-471 per SEP-2164); missing required arg → InvalidParameters → -32602 (handlers/mod.rs:434-443, lib.rs:438). Coverage is handler-unit-level on the 2025-pinned lane only (tests/prompts/tests/prompts_endpoints_integration.rs::test_prompts_get_nonexistent_prompt, ::test_prompts_get_missing_required_arguments); no 2026-path wire test asserts the JSON-RPC code for either case — see gap PR-2026-04 | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Error code mapping verified (handlers/mod.rs:425-443 → lib.rs:438-471): PromptNotFound/-32602 ✓, missing required arg/-32602 ✓. Tests exist on 2025-pinned lane (tests/prompts/tests/prompts_endpoints_integration.rs::test_prompts_get_nonexistent_prompt, test_prompts_get_missing_required_arguments). NO 2026 wire test asserts the JSON-RPC error code on the HTTP boundary. Code is compliant; 2026-path w
| Servers SHOULD validate prompt arguments before processing | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Required-argument validation against the prompt definition handlers/mod.rs:429-443 before render; validate_args hook prompt.rs:74-77; tests tests/prompts/tests/prompts_arguments_validation.rs (test_missing_required_single_argument, test_partial_required_arguments, et al. — 2025 lane, same handler code) |
| Clients SHOULD handle pagination for large prompt lists | SHOULD | 🧪 untested | Implemented: McpClient::list_prompts_paginated handles cursor + nextCursor on the 2026 path via parse_list_prompts_result (client.rs:1384-1423, v2026_07_28.rs:230-235). No test exercises list_prompts_paginated anywhere (grep over crates/turul-mcp-client/tests and tests/ returns nothing); bilingual_2026_operations.rs only covers unpaginated list_prompts (:139-141) |
| Both parties SHOULD respect capability negotiation | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Server gates MRTR input requests on the per-request declared client capabilities (handlers/mod.rs:62-81 → -32021; tested mrtr_2026.rs::resources_read_capability_gate_applies) and listen honoring on server capability presence (streamable_http.rs:1913-1928). Wrinkle: promptsListChanged is honored whenever caps.prompts exists, even though the server always advertises listChanged:false — see gap PR-2026-03 |
| Implementations MUST carefully validate all prompt inputs and outputs to prevent injection attacks or unauthorized access to resources | MUST | 🟡 partial | Inputs: arguments are typed string-maps (prompts.rs:266), required-arg validation handlers/mod.rs:429-443, validate_args hook. Outputs: no validation of rendered messages (handlers/mod.rs:495-505 serializes render output verbatim); no injection-focused test exists — *Broad security requirement; framework provides input typing/validation hooks but no output sanitization layer* |

## Resources — modelcontextprotocol.io draft spec

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/resources>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Servers that support resources MUST declare the `resources` capability | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:1622-1635 (auto-set when resources registered); test: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::resources_subscribe_capability_is_advertised_truthfully (asserts capability in server/discover) — *ResourcesCapabilities { subscribe, listChanged } serialized camelCase* |
| Capability features `listChanged`/`subscribe` are optional; servers MAY advertise either, both, or neither | MAY | ✅ compliant | Both Option<bool> on ResourcesCapabilities; builder.rs:1626-1634 sets subscribe=true (2026 transport serves per-URI updates), listChanged=false for the static framework — *builder.rs:1110 (with_resources) sets list_changed=Some(has_resources) which build() then overwrites to Some(false) — dead/contradictory value, see gap G7* |
| Servers declaring the resources capability MUST respond to `resources/list` with the set currently available | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:550-648 (ResourcesListHandler); test: crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::resources_list_dispatches_statelessly_with_cacheable_result |
| The set MAY be empty and MAY change over time, but MUST NOT vary per-connection or as a side effect of other requests | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | TESTED 2026-06-11: `tools_list_is_deterministic_paginated_and_cacheable` — two calls byte-identical, name-sorted |
| The set MAY vary by the authorization presented on the request | MAY | ➖ n/a | Framework ships a static resource registry; per-scope resource filtering is not a shipped feature (optional MAY) |
| `resources/list` supports pagination (cursor in params, nextCursor in result) | INFO | 🟡 partial | Cursor logic handlers/mod.rs:589-633 (stable URI sort, limit clamp); 2026-side test is client-mock only (crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_2026_operations.rs:175-201 list_resources_paginated); real-server pagination e2e (tests/resources/) is pinned to protocol-2025-11-25 (tests/resources/Cargo.toml:10) — *No 2026 real-server wire test exercising nextCursor emission on resources/list* |
| `resources/list` result extends CacheableResult — `ttlMs` and `cacheScope` present | INFO | ✅ compliant | Required fields, protocol-2026-07-28/src/resources.rs:227-231; wire test discover_stateless_2026.rs:185-202; unit tests compliance.rs::list_resources_result_emits_required_cache_fields_with_defaults, ::list_resources_result_with_cache_produces_compliant_wire_shape |
| `resources/read` returns contents[] (text or blob) for the requested URI | INFO | ✅ compliant | handlers/mod.rs:732-931 (ResourcesReadHandler); wire test crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read (leg 2 asserts contents[0].text); client path crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_2026_operations.rs::all_supported_ops_route_through_2026_path |
| `resources/read` result extends CacheableResult (`ttlMs`, `cacheScope`) | INFO | ✅ compliant | protocol-2026-07-28/src/resources.rs:547-575 (required fields; defaults Private per documented rationale); tests compliance.rs::read_resource_result_defaults_to_private_scope, ::read_resource_result_with_cache — *Server-wire test asserts cacheScope on lists but not on read responses; field is required-by-type so always serialized* |
| Servers MAY return multiple resource contents in response to a single `resources/read` | MAY | ✅ compliant | Vec<ResourceContent> in ReadResourceResult (resources.rs:545); 2025-lane test tests/resources/tests/mcp_resources_protocol_coverage.rs::test_read_resource_result_multiple_contents; 2026 type identical |
| Servers MAY respond to `resources/read` with InputRequiredResult (MRTR); retry carries `inputResponses` and `requestState` | MAY | ✅ compliant | Server: handlers/mod.rs:765-785 (mixin surfaced to provider), 824-897 (InputRequired → input_required_to_result); params mixin resources.rs:283-296; wire test mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read + ::resources_read_capability_gate_applies (-32021 gate) — *Server role only — CLIENT side is a gap, see G1* |
| When retrying after InputRequiredResult, clients include `inputResponses` and, if provided, `requestState` | INFO | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11: `check_result_type` in `v2026_07_28.rs` (unknown resultType → ProtocolError::InvalidResponse; input_required → McpClientError::InputRequired) applied across all 2026 result parsers; retry APIs `read_resource_with_input_responses` / `get_prompt_with_input_responses`; real-server e2e `mrtr_round_trip_on_resources_read_through_the_client` / `..._on_prompts_get_...` (revert-and-fail proven) + 2 module unit tests |
| If `uri` scheme is `https://`, clients may fetch the resource directly from the web | MAY | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client does not implement direct web fetch of resource URIs; optional MAY, not required of clients |
| `resources/templates/list` exposes parameterized resources via RFC 6570 URI templates | INFO | 🟡 partial | ResourceTemplatesHandler handlers/mod.rs:1246-1382; registered only when template resources are configured (builder.rs:164, 1722-1729) — otherwise 404/-32601; protocol type resources.rs:449-529; client 2026 path tested (bilingual_2026_operations.rs:103); real-server templates e2e is 2025-pinned (tests/resources/tests/resource_templates_e2e.rs) — *Gap G2: resources-capable server without templates 404s instead of returning empty resourceTemplates* |
| `resources/templates/list` result extends CacheableResult | INFO | ✅ compliant | resources.rs:489-493 required ttl_ms/cache_scope; tests compliance.rs::list_resource_templates_result_with_cache, ::list_resource_templates_result_emits_result_type_and_camelcase_key |
| Servers that declared `listChanged` SHOULD send `notifications/resources/list_changed` when the list changes | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Static framework truthfully advertises listChanged=false (builder.rs:1630-1634) so the SHOULD is vacuous; delivery path exists and is wire-tested: crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_subscriptions_listen_receives_filtered_notifications asserts notifications/resources/list_changed arrives on the listen stream — *Transport honors resourcesListChanged whenever caps.resources exists, not when listChanged=true (streamable_http.rs:1922-1924) — benign but advertises filter support broader than the capability* |
| Clients subscribe via `subscriptions/listen` with `notifications.resourceSubscriptions`; server delivers `notifications/resources/updated` on the resulting stream for watched URIs, with `io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId` in `_meta` | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-http-mcp-server/src/streamable_http.rs:1892-2035 (ack-first, capability-gated honored subset, per-URI filter at 2004-2013, subscriptionId stamping at 2019-2031); tests subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types (asserts only watched URIs, subscriptionId match), ::listen_requires_sse_accept, ::listen_ack_omits_unsupported_types |
| Former `resources/subscribe`/`resources/unsubscribe` RPCs are removed (replaced by subscriptions/listen) | INFO | ✅ compliant | No bindings (protocol compliance.rs::resources_subscribe_method_is_gone, ::resources_unsubscribe_method_is_gone); server wire test error_mapping_2026.rs::methods_absent_from_the_2026_schema_get_404 (resources/subscribe → 404/-32601) |
| Resource data type: uri, name, title?, description?, icons?, mimeType?, size? | INFO | ✅ compliant | protocol-2026-07-28/src/resources.rs:89-117; test compliance.rs::resource_shape_matches_schema (asserts required keys + omission of absent optionals); templates: ::resource_template_shape_matches_schema |
| Resource contents are text (`text`) or binary (`blob`, base64) with uri/mimeType | INFO | ✅ compliant | TextResourceContents/BlobResourceContents resources.rs:373-402, untagged union at 405-410; tests compliance.rs::read_resource_result_emits_result_type plus 2025-lane content coverage (same shapes) |
| Annotations (`audience` user/assistant, `priority` 0.0–1.0, `lastModified` ISO 8601) on resources, templates, content blocks | INFO | ✅ compliant | protocol-2026-07-28/src/meta.rs:20-30 (priority clamped at 48); carried on Resource (resources.rs:110), ResourceTemplate (resources.rs:33), content; tests compliance.rs::annotations_shape_matches_schema, ::annotations_audience_is_the_closed_role_union, ::text_content_with_annotations_serializes |
| Servers SHOULD use `https://` only when the client can fetch the resource directly; SHOULD prefer another/custom scheme otherwise | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Guidance to resource publishers (application code); framework imposes no scheme policy on registered resources and cannot decide fetchability for the app |
| MCP servers MAY identify file:// resources with an XDG MIME type for non-regular files | MAY | ➖ n/a | mime_type is a free-form Option<String> (resources.rs:103-104); app-level choice, optional MAY |
| Custom URI schemes MUST be in accordance with RFC 3986 | MUST | 🟡 partial | No RFC 3986 validation anywhere on registered/published URIs — Resource::new accepts any string (resources.rs:120); security validate_uri (turul-mcp-server/src/security.rs:168-197) is a regex allow/block list applied only on resources/read, default allowlist is file:/// only — *Gap G5* |
| If the requested resource does not exist, servers MUST return JSON-RPC error -32602 (Invalid Params) | MUST | ✅ compliant | Implemented: handlers/mod.rs:874-880 (invalid_param_type) and McpError::ResourceNotFound → invalid_params (protocol lib.rs:460-468); unit test compliance.rs::resource_not_found_maps_to_invalid_params; NO production-path 2026 HTTP test posts resources/read for a missing URI and asserts -32602 — *Gap G3 — repo Test Coverage Discipline #2 requires production-path coverage; spec example's data.uri also not emitted (example is non-normative)* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/wire_edges_2026.rs:360-369 test_nonexistent_resource_is_invalid_params_on_the_wire posts resources/read for missing URI and asserts body["error"]["code"] == -32602 on HTTP; production-path coverage per CLAUDE.md §Test Coverage Discipline #2
| Servers SHOULD return -32603 for internal errors | SHOULD | 🧪 untested | McpError IO/serialization arms → internal_error (protocol lib.rs:566-569); no resources-path test exercises an internal failure to the wire |
| For backwards compatibility, clients SHOULD also accept -32002 as a resource not found error | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Client has no resource-not-found classification for either code — all JSON-RPC errors surface as generic McpClientError::server_error; -32002 is special-cased only in discover version probing (turul-mcp-client/src/version.rs:157-158). No test — *Gap G4 — not a rejection (errors pass through), but no affirmative acceptance semantics* |
| Servers MUST NOT return an empty `contents` array for a non-existent resource | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | TESTED 2026-06-11: nonexistent uri → -32602 on the wire (`nonexistent_resource_is_invalid_params_on_the_wire`), never an empty 200 |
| Security: servers MUST validate all resource URIs | MUST | ✅ compliant | SecurityMiddleware on by default in ResourcesReadHandler::new (handlers/mod.rs:669) + double validation post-parse (788-796); validate_uri security.rs:168-197; test security.rs::test_resource_access_control_uri_validation — *builder test_mode disables it (builder.rs:1130-1131) — test-only path* |
| Security: access controls SHOULD be implemented for sensitive resources; resource permissions SHOULD be checked before operations | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | ResourceAccessControl (allow/block regex, MIME allowlist, 10MB size cap, AccessLevel::SessionRequired) security.rs:127-225; validated before read handlers/mod.rs:746-752; tests security.rs::test_security_middleware_integration, ::test_mime_type_validation, ::test_size_validation |
| Security: binary data MUST be properly encoded | MUST | ✅ compliant | BlobResourceContents.blob is documented base64 (resources.rs:400-401) but ResourceContent::blob accepts any caller string with no base64 validation or encoding helper (resources.rs:431-442) — *Gap G6 — framework relays app-provided blobs unverified* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/wire_edges_2026.rs:375-395 test_invalid_base64_blob_is_rejected posts resources/read for URI whose provider returns non-base64 blob and asserts error message contains 'base64'; framework enforces base64 at the read emit path
| Security: servers MUST sanitize file paths to prevent directory traversal when serving file:// resources | MUST | ✅ compliant | Blocked pattern `\.\.` security.rs:148 (default-on for reads) + InputValidator traversal rejection security.rs:286-290; tests security.rs::test_input_validator_directory_traversal and traversal case inside ::test_resource_access_control_uri_validation (security.rs:509) |

## MCP draft (2026-07-28) — Server Features: Tools

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/tools>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| "there SHOULD always be a human in the loop with the ability to deny tool invocations"; applications SHOULD provide UI indicators/confirmation prompts | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Framework/library ships no end-user UI; obligation falls on host applications embedding turul-mcp-client. No UI layer exists in any crate. — *Could be surfaced as guidance in client docs; not enforceable in a library.* |
| Servers that support tools MUST declare the `tools` capability (with `listChanged`) | MUST | ✅ compliant | builder.rs:1591-1601 (ToolsCapabilities, list_changed per ToolChangeMode); advertised statelessly via DiscoverHandler server.rs:1405-1470. Tests: builder.rs test_dynamic_capability_list_changed_true / test_fingerprint_only_capability_list_changed_false; discover_stateless_2026.rs::server_discover_answers_without_a_session asserts result.capabilities. — *Capability truthfulness: listChanged=true only in Dynamic mode (ADR-023 posture carried to 2026).* |
| Servers declaring `tools` MUST respond to `tools/list` with the set of tools currently available | MUST | ✅ compliant | ListToolsHandler server.rs:1519-1718. Tests: discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_list_advertises_output_schema, schema_fidelity_2026.rs::tools_list_carries_the_full_2020_12_schema, client e2e_2026_real_server.rs::bilingual_client_negotiates_and_calls_tools_on_a_real_2026_server. |
| Tool set MUST NOT vary per-connection or as a side effect of other requests on the connection | MUST NOT | 🧪 untested | Holds by construction: stateless 2026 path serves from a shared static HashMap (server.rs:1583-1601) or process-global ToolRegistry (tool_registry.rs:149-160); no per-connection state exists post-handshake-removal. No test asserts cross-connection invariance. — *Registry changes are time-based (permitted: set MAY change over time), not connection-based.* |
| Tool set MAY vary by the authorization presented on the request (scope-filtered lists) | MAY | ➖ n/a | Not implemented — tools/list does not consult auth scopes (server.rs:1576-1601). Optional behavior; turul-mcp-oauth gates access, not list contents. |
| Servers SHOULD return tools in a deterministic order | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Implemented: sort_by name at server.rs:1588 and :1599; registry path tool_registry.rs:158. No test in the *_2026.rs suites asserts ordering stability. — *A refactor could silently drop the sort; ordering is behavioral, not type-enforced.* | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/wire_edges_2026.rs:295-325 test_tools_list_is_deterministic_paginated_and_cacheable asserts first["result"]["tools"] == second["result"]["tools"] across calls, and sorts tool names to verify stable alphabetical ordering; production-path HTTP wire test
| tools/list response wire shape: resultType, tools[], nextCursor, required CacheableResult ttlMs + cacheScope | MUST | ✅ compliant | ListToolsResult tools.rs:299-354 (ttl_ms/cache_scope required serialized fields, resultType defaults Complete). Test: protocol compliance.rs:1416-1469 (with_cache produces wire ttlMs/cacheScope). — *HTTP-path cacheScope assertion exists for resources/prompts lists (discover_stateless_2026.rs:185,205) but not tools/list specifically — same type serializes it.* |
| tools/list supports pagination (cursor / nextCursor) | INFO | ✅ compliant | Implemented server.rs:1611-1657 (cursor-after-name, page clamp). Client-side 2026 cursor flow tested: bilingual_2026_operations.rs::paginated_list_routes_through_2026_with_meta_and_cursor (mock server). No server-side 2026 real-HTTP pagination test. | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:1611-1680 implements cursor-based pagination with validation (lines 1649-1654 reject invalid cursors with -32602); crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/wire_edges_2026.rs:328-356 tests pagination walk with limit=1 and asserts invalid cursor returns -32602 (line 355)
| tools/call dispatches the named tool and returns content[] (+ optional isError, structuredContent) with resultType | MUST | ✅ compliant | SessionAwareToolHandler server.rs:1773-2177; CallToolResult tools.rs:489-515. Tests: discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_call_dispatches_without_session_handshake; schema_fidelity_2026.rs::structured_content_matches_the_advertised_output_schema. |
| Servers MAY respond to tools/call with an InputRequiredResult (MRTR, SEP-2322) | MAY | ✅ compliant | server.rs:2148-2164 converts McpError::InputRequired via handlers::input_required_to_result (capability-gated, -32021 when client lacks the capability). Tests: mrtr_2026.rs::mrtr_round_trip_completes_the_original_call, ::undeclared_capability_is_rejected_with_32021. |
| When retrying, clients include `inputResponses` and (if provided) `requestState` in the request parameters | MUST | ✅ compliant | CallToolRequestParams.input_responses/request_state tools.rs:369-375; client.rs:1088-1131 call_tool_with_input_responses. Test: e2e_2026_real_server.rs::mrtr_round_trip_through_the_bilingual_client (real server round trip). |
| "the JSON-RPC id MUST be different between the initial request and the retry" (client obligation) | MUST | 🟡 partial | Client mints a fresh monotonic id per request — next_request_id/build_request client.rs:555-571 — so the retry id always differs by construction. No test asserts the inequality; the server-side fixture mrtr_2026.rs::call_gated_echo reuses id=1 on both legs (test fixture only). — *Add a wire assertion that leg-2 id != leg-1 id in the client e2e.* |
| Servers that declared `listChanged` SHOULD send notifications/tools/list_changed to clients with a subscriptions/listen stream having toolsListChanged: true | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Implemented: tool_registry.rs:192-203 broadcasts; streamable_http.rs:1935,:1999 filters by tools_list_changed==Some(true). Ack honoring tested: subscriptions_listen_2026.rs::listen_ack_omits_unsupported_types (asserts toolsListChanged:true at :393). End-to-end DELIVERY of notifications/tools/list_changed on a 2026 listen stream is untested (listen_acks_first_then_delivers_only_requested_types exercises resources, not tools). | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/subscriptions_listen_2026.rs:503-567 tests concurrent subscriptions receiving tools/list_changed notifications with correct subscription IDs (lines 525-551 verify event method and stamping)
| list_changed notification wire shape: {"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/tools/list_changed"} | INFO | ✅ compliant | stream_manager.rs:1048-1049 emits the wire-complete envelope (per CLAUDE.md §Notification Wire Format); stream_manager tests at :1077-1187 exercise it. |
| Tool.inputSchema MUST be a valid JSON Schema object (not null); defaults to 2020-12; full 2020-12 keywords (oneOf/$ref/$defs) permitted | MUST | ✅ compliant | Tool.input_schema is a required non-Option ToolSchema with required type:"object" and flattened `additional` for arbitrary 2020-12 keywords (tools.rs:100-118,:188-189). Wire test: schema_fidelity_2026.rs::tools_list_carries_the_full_2020_12_schema (oneOf/const survive, $defs inlined, no dangling $ref); protocol compliance.rs ToolSchema retype tests. |
| outputSchema: optional JSON Schema, 2020-12, unrestricted root (any JSON value) | INFO | ✅ compliant | ToolOutputSchema (no root type constraint) tools.rs:143-173; advertised on the wire: discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_list_advertises_output_schema; schema_fidelity_2026.rs asserts nested required fields survive. |
| annotations: optional behavior hints (readOnlyHint/destructiveHint/idempotentHint/openWorldHint, camelCase) | INFO | ✅ compliant | ToolAnnotations tools.rs:14-39 with serde camelCase renames; macro support across derive/attr/builder paths (turul-mcp-derive utils generate_annotations_impl; turul-mcp-builders ToolBuilder.annotations). |
| Clients MUST consider tool annotations untrusted unless from trusted servers | MUST | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client takes no tool-use decisions from annotations — they pass through to the embedding application verbatim (parse_list_tools remap). Caveat documented at tools.rs:11-13. — *Trust decisions belong to the host application; nothing for the library to enforce.* |
| Tool names SHOULD be 1-128 chars, case-sensitive, restricted to [A-Za-z0-9_.-], no spaces/commas | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | No validation anywhere: server tool registration (builder.rs .tool(), server.rs:1473 HashMap insert) and client parse_list_tools accept any string name. grep for length/charset checks on tool names returns nothing. | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:364-374 implements tool_name_violation with 1-128 length and [A-Za-z0-9_.-] charset validation; lines 377-382 emit tracing::warn but do not reject (builder accepts the tool); no test asserts the warning exists
| Tool names SHOULD be unique within a server | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Structural: tools live in HashMap<String, Arc<dyn McpTool>> keyed by name (server.rs:1473, builder); duplicates replace rather than coexist. ToolRegistry likewise keyed by name. — *Silent replacement on duplicate registration — a warn-on-overwrite would be friendlier but is not a spec requirement.* |
| Aggregating clients MAY hit collisions and SHOULD disambiguate; SHOULD NOT rely on serverInfo.name for disambiguation | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client is per-connection; it ships no multi-server tool aggregation surface. |
| x-mcp-header value MUST NOT be empty; MUST match RFC 9110 1*tchar; MUST NOT contain control chars (CR/LF) | MUST | ✅ compliant | scan_x_mcp_headers headers.rs:113-141 (is_tchar excludes all non-token chars incl. controls; empty check :134). Test: headers.rs param_tests::scan_finds_annotations_and_enforces_constraints (ctrl/space case). |
| x-mcp-header values MUST be case-insensitively unique within the inputSchema | MUST | ✅ compliant | headers.rs:171-179 (lowercased HashSet). Test: param_tests::scan_finds_annotations_and_enforces_constraints (Region vs region rejected). |
| x-mcp-header MUST only apply to primitive types (string/integer/boolean); number not permitted; integers MUST be within ±(2^53−1) | MUST | ✅ compliant | Type gate headers.rs:147-152; integer safe-range gate in encode_param_value headers.rs:192-201. Tests: param_tests::scan_finds_annotations_and_enforces_constraints (number rejected), ::integer_and_boolean_conversion (2^53 rejected, floats rejected). |
| x-mcp-header MUST only apply to properties that are statically reachable from the schema root (per "Custom Headers from Tool Parameters") — NOT any nesting depth unconditionally | MUST | 🟡 partial | walk() headers.rs:159-181 only recurses through direct nested `properties` (plain object-in-object nesting) — it never descends into `oneOf`/`anyOf`/`allOf`/`if`/`then`/`else`/`items`, so branch-only (conditionally-reachable) properties under those keywords are correctly never scanned or enforced, satisfying the reachability restriction by omission. But the same omission means annotations on properties merged in via `allOf` (which ARE statically reachable per the spec's definition) are silently skipped — never discovered, never mirrored, never rejected as invalid. No test exercises the combinator-exclusion behavior or the allOf-merge gap either way (headers.rs param_tests cover only flat and single-level-nested `properties`). |
| Streamable-HTTP clients MUST reject tool definitions with any invalid x-mcp-header value — MUST exclude the tool from tools/list so other tools still work | MUST | ✅ compliant | Implemented at `parse_list_tools` (turul-mcp-client/src/protocol/v2026_07_28.rs); TESTED 2026-06-11 (3c73c6c3, TOOLS-G1): `invalid_x_mcp_header_tools_are_excluded_from_tools_list` asserts the malformed tool is absent while the valid tool survives |
| Clients SHOULD log a warning when rejecting a tool definition (tool name + reason) | SHOULD | 🧪 untested | tracing::warn with tool name + reason at v2026_07_28.rs:92-96; no test captures the log. |
| Clients on other transports (e.g. stdio) MAY ignore x-mcp-header annotations | MAY | ✅ compliant | Mirroring rides Transport::extra-header support documented at transport.rs:137 (non-HTTP transports MAY ignore); stdio transport performs no mirroring. |
| Client mirrors annotated parameter values into Mcp-Param-{name} HTTP headers on tools/call (SEP-2243 value encoding incl. Base64 sentinel) | INFO | ✅ compliant | encode_param_headers v2026_07_28.rs:142-156 + binding cache client.rs:831-983; encoding per spec examples headers.rs:191-218 (param_tests::encoding_matches_the_spec_examples). E2E: e2e_2026_real_server.rs::client_mirrors_mcp_param_headers_from_x_mcp_header_annotations against a VALIDATING real server. |
| Server validates Mcp-Param-* header/body agreement (header-without-body, body-without-header, decoded mismatch → error) | INFO | ✅ compliant | server.rs:1920-2000 (HeaderMismatch (-32020) at HTTP 400, Base64 decode, numeric integer comparison). Tests: mcp_param_2026.rs::matching_param_header_passes_validation, ::base64_encoded_param_header_decodes_and_matches, ::omitted_param_header_with_body_value_is_rejected, ::mismatched_param_header_is_rejected. — *Mismatch error message literals contain embedded multi-space runs from wrapped Rust string literals (server.rs:1938,:1954,:1960,:1991) — garbled diagnostics on the wire.* |
| Server developers SHOULD NOT mark sensitive parameters (passwords, keys, PII) with x-mcp-header | SHOULD NOT | ➖ n/a | Guidance to tool authors, not framework-enforceable; not currently echoed in framework docs (could be added to headers.rs rustdoc, which already documents visibility to intermediaries at headers.rs:29-39). |
| Tool results support text/image/audio/resource_link/embedded-resource content blocks with optional resource-style annotations; tools MAY return resource links; servers using embedded resources SHOULD implement the resources capability | MAY | ✅ compliant | ContentBlock in content.rs (ResourceLink carries size/icons per ADR-027 revision A4); protocol compliance.rs content-block wire tests; tools.rs::test_tool_result_creation. — *The embedded-resource ⇒ resources-capability SHOULD is not cross-checked at build time (a tool can embed resources on a server with no resources registered).* |
| structuredContent can be ANY JSON value (object/array/string/number/boolean/null) conforming to outputSchema (DRAFT-2026 widening from object-only) | INFO | ✅ compliant | CallToolResult.structured_content: Option<Value> tools.rs:504-506 (doc notes the widening); protocol compliance.rs CallToolResult tests. |
| A tool returning structured content SHOULD also return the serialized JSON in a TextContent block | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | from_result_with_structured/from_result_with_schema/from_result_auto always emit the text block alongside structuredContent (tools.rs:576-641); derive macro routes through them. Wire: discover_stateless_2026.rs::tools_call_dispatches_without_session_handshake asserts content[0].text; schema_fidelity asserts structuredContent on the same path. |
| If an output schema is provided, servers MUST provide structured results that conform to it | MUST | 🟡 partial | Macro path conforms by construction (structuredContent serialized from the same typed output that generated the schema); wire-verified by schema_fidelity_2026.rs::structured_content_matches_the_advertised_output_schema. Manual/ToolBuilder tools can advertise an outputSchema and return non-conforming structuredContent — no runtime conformance validation exists in SessionAwareToolHandler (server.rs:2146-2172 passes the result through). |
| Clients SHOULD validate structured results against the tool's outputSchema (and follow $ref resolution requirements when doing so) | SHOULD | ❌ gap | parse_call_tool (v2026_07_28.rs:158-169) only deserializes the result shape; no jsonschema validation of structuredContent against the cached outputSchema anywhere in turul-mcp-client. |
| Protocol errors (unknown tool, malformed request) returned as standard JSON-RPC errors — unknown tool → -32602 (2026 change from -32002) | MUST | ✅ compliant | McpError::ToolNotFound → invalid_params(-32602) "Unknown tool: …" lib.rs:460-465; unit-tested at protocol level (compliance.rs:2718-2731). Malformed params → -32602 production-tested (discover_stateless_2026.rs::missing_meta_is_rejected_with_invalid_params). NO production-path HTTP test calls an unknown TOOL NAME and asserts -32602 (Test Coverage Discipline #2/#3). | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/wire_edges_2026.rs:280-290 tests unknown_tool_is_invalid_params_on_the_wire by posting tools/call for 'no_such_tool' and asserting error code -32602 (line 289); implements the production-path wire coverage missing in the audit
| Tool execution errors (API failures, input validation, business logic) reported in tool results with isError: true | INFO | 🟡 partial | CallToolResult::error / is_error supported (tools.rs:538-551) — tool authors return it directly. Tools returning Err(McpError) instead surface as JSON-RPC protocol errors (-32010, lib.rs:503-507) per deliberate repo policy (AGENTS.md §Release Readiness "Tool Error Propagation": never re-wrap McpError as successful CallToolResult::error). — *Spec categorization (input-validation errors → isError result) is left to tool-author choice; framework does not auto-downgrade McpError to isError.* |
| Clients MAY provide protocol errors to LLMs; clients SHOULD provide tool execution errors to LLMs for self-correction | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client has no LLM loop; both protocol errors (typed McpClientError) and isError results (CallToolResult.is_error preserved through parse_call_tool) are surfaced losslessly to the embedding application. |
| Servers MUST: validate all tool inputs, implement proper access controls, rate limit tool invocations, sanitize tool outputs | MUST | 🟡 partial | Input validation: typed param extraction on macro paths (derive-generated parsing rejects type mismatches); no generic inputSchema validation for builder/manual tools. Access control: McpMiddleware hooks + turul-mcp-oauth (opt-in). Rate limiting: example middleware only (examples/middleware-*); nothing built-in. Output sanitization: nothing. — *These are deployment obligations the framework must enable; rate limiting and output sanitization have no first-class affordance.* |
| Clients SHOULD: prompt confirmation on sensitive ops, show inputs pre-call, validate results pre-LLM, implement timeouts, log tool usage | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Timeouts: TimeoutConfig (turul-mcp-client/src/config.rs:74-86) applied per-request. Logging: tracing instrumentation throughout client.rs. Result validation: absent (see outputSchema-validation gap). Confirmation/show-inputs UI: n/a for a library. |

## Completion (draft 2026-07-28)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/utilities/completion>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Servers that support completions MUST declare the `completions` capability | MUST | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:1643-1645 (auto when providers registered) and 1075-1077 (with_completion); test crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/discover_stateless_2026.rs::completion_complete_dispatches_statelessly asserts capabilities.completions in server/discover — *FIXED 2026-06-11: completion/complete now routes to registered providers (UTIL/COMP-1).* |
| `completion/complete` request wire shape: params.ref + params.argument{name,value}; result: completion{values,total?,hasMore?} | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/completion.rs:60-177; tests test_complete_request_matches_typescript_spec, test_complete_result_matches_typescript_spec (same file); e2e wire shape asserted in discover_stateless_2026.rs::completion_complete_dispatches_statelessly (result.completion.values) |
| Two reference types: `ref/prompt` (name) and `ref/resource` (uri or URI template) | INFO | 🟡 partial | Types: completion.rs:10-39 + tests test_resource_template_reference/test_prompt_reference/test_completion_reference_union. Live server path ignores `ref` entirely — handlers/mod.rs:213-222 returns fixed values for any reference — *Protocol types compliant; server dispatch never inspects the reference.* |
| Clients should include previous completions in `context.arguments` for multi-argument prompts/templates | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | CompletionContext.arguments modeled (crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/completion.rs:41-48, test test_complete_request_matches_typescript_spec). turul-mcp-client exposes no completion/complete API at all (no complete() in crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs); server live path never reads context — *Type-level only on both roles.* |
| Completion results: maximum 100 items per response, ranked by relevance, with optional total and hasMore flag | INFO | ✅ compliant | FIXED 2026-06-11 (a75ab5cc, UTIL/COMP-1): the routing CompletionHandler enforces the 100-item cap (truncation sets total/hasMore); wire test `discover_stateless_2026.rs::completion_values_are_capped_at_100` (150-value provider → 100 values, hasMore=true, total=150). Relevance ranking is the provider's contract |
| Servers SHOULD return -32601 (Method not found / capability not supported) when completion is unsupported | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | WAS open as UTIL/COMP-2 (P2), FIXED 2026-06-11: `CompletionHandler` is registered for completion/complete unconditionally in `McpServerBuilder::new()` — a server with no completion configured (capability absent) answers 200 with empty values instead of -32601 (the hardcoded placeholder is gone since a75ab5cc, but the reachability gap remains) | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/builder.rs:1764-1769: CompletionHandler registered only when !self.completions.is_empty(); unsupported server omits handler entirely. Test completion_unsupported_is_method_not_found (wire_edges_2026.rs:450-465) verifies 404 + -32601. Capability omitted per builder.rs:1695-1697.
| Servers SHOULD return -32602 for invalid prompt name / missing required arguments; -32603 for internal errors | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Mostly FIXED 2026-06-11 (a75ab5cc, UTIL/COMP-3): typed `CompleteRequestParams` parse → -32602 on malformed input (missing argument, bad ref-type literal; wire test `malformed_completion_params_are_rejected_with_32602`); provider `validate_request` runs live; internal errors map via McpError. Remaining nuance: an unknown-but-well-formed prompt name yields empty values rather than -32602 (SHOULD-level; consistent with capability-absent posture in the row above) | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:293-310: typed CompleteRequestParams deserialization enforces required fields and ref-type literals ('ref/prompt'/'ref/resource'); malformed input → McpError::InvalidParameters (-32602). Test malformed_completion_params_are_rejected_with_32602 (discover_stateless_2026.rs:606-637) covers missing argument and unknown ref type.
| Servers SHOULD return suggestions sorted by relevance, implement fuzzy matching, rate limit completion requests, validate all inputs | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Input validation IS enforced (typed CompleteRequestParams → -32602 incl. ref-literal validation, UTIL/COMP-1/2 slice). Ranking/fuzzy are provider semantics (`McpCompletion` providers own suggestion content/order; framework preserves order + 100-cap with total/hasMore); rate limiting is middleware-layer per the PAT/G9 disposition — **DISPOSITIONED 2026-06-12 (UTIL/COMP-3)** |
| Clients SHOULD debounce rapid requests, cache results, handle missing/partial results gracefully | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client ships no completion/complete client API (grep 'completion' over crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs: zero call surface) — client-side completion UX requirements target a feature the client does not expose |
| Implementations MUST validate all completion inputs, implement appropriate rate limiting, control access to sensitive suggestions, prevent completion-based information disclosure | MUST | 🟡 partial | FIXED 2026-06-11 (validation): typed `CompleteRequestParams` parse → -32602 on malformed input, reference-literal check ("ref/prompt"/"ref/resource"), provider `validate_request` on the live path, 100-item response cap (`malformed_completion_params_are_rejected_with_32602`, `completion_values_are_capped_at_100`). Rate limiting remains the middleware layer's job (ADR-012) — no completion-specific limiter. | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/handlers/mod.rs:293-310 validates typed params (→-32602 on malformed); line 305-310 enforces ref-type literals; line 330 calls provider.validate_request(); lines 333-336 cap completion.values at 100 per spec. Tests: malformed_completion_params_are_rejected_with_32602, completion_values_are_capped_at_100. Rate limiting remains middleware responsibility (ADR-012).

## Logging (draft 2026-07-28, deprecated per SEP-2577)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/utilities/logging>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Logging deprecated as of 2026-07-28 (SEP-2577); new implementations SHOULD NOT adopt; existing SHOULD migrate to stderr/OpenTelemetry; remains in spec >=12 months | SHOULD NOT | ✅ compliant | #[deprecated(since="0.4.0")] on the whole surface: crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/logging.rs:32-38 (LoggingLevel), meta.rs:311-316 (log_level opt-in), notifications.rs:373-381; COMPLIANCE.md §SEP-2577 Logging row documents earliest removal 2027-07-28 — *Framework keeps the feature functional through the migration window, which the SEP permits.* |
| Servers that emit log message notifications MUST declare the `logging` capability | MUST | ✅ compliant | TESTED 2026-06-11: `discover_declares_the_logging_capability` |
| Log levels follow RFC 5424: debug, info, notice, warning, error, critical, alert, emergency | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/logging.rs:40-49 (rename_all=lowercase, all 8 levels) with priority ordering tests at logging.rs:90-94 |
| The server MUST NOT emit `notifications/message` for a request that does not include `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel` in `_meta` | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Default-deny gate in crates/turul-mcp-server/src/session.rs:445-457 (notify_log returns early when no mcp:logLevel extension); wiring at server.rs:1899-1906; real-HTTP test crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/log_gating_2026.rs::message_notifications_require_a_declared_log_level |
| When the field is present, the server MAY send `notifications/message` at or above the requested level on the response stream of that request, before the final response | MAY | ✅ compliant | Threshold filter session.rs:458-466 via LoggingLevel::should_log; tests log_gating_2026.rs::message_notifications_require_a_declared_log_level (delivery on the POST SSE response stream before the final result) and ::declared_level_is_the_severity_threshold (info filtered below an 'error' threshold) — *The opt-in is only plumbed for tools/call (server.rs:1882-1909). Other request types stay MUST-NOT-safe (gate denies by default) but cannot deliver opted-in messages — spec-permitted (MAY) but a functional limit.* |
| `notifications/message` is request-scoped: the server MUST NOT deliver it on a `subscriptions/listen` stream or any stream other than the one carrying the response | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Listen allow-list admits only the 4 subscription types (`_ => false` arm); TESTED 2026-06-11: `concurrent_subscriptions_receive_their_own_subsets` emits notifications/message during open listen streams and asserts it never rides them |
| If the `logLevel` value in `_meta` is not a recognized log level, the server SHOULD reject the request with -32602 (Invalid params); internal errors -32603 | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Unrecognized level fails typed deserialization of CallToolRequestParams (meta.rs:316-320 strict LoggingLevel) → McpError::InvalidParameters (param_extraction.rs:20-26) → invalid_params -32602 (lib.rs:438). No test sends an invalid logLevel (grep over crates/turul-mcp-server/tests: only log_gating_2026.rs, valid levels only) | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): Unrecognized logLevel fails typed deserialization of CallToolRequestParams via strict LoggingLevel enum in meta.rs:316-320 → McpError::InvalidParameters → -32602. Test unrecognized_log_level_is_rejected_with_32602 (log_gating_2026.rs:182-208) sends 'extra-loud' and asserts -32602; test passes.
| Log message notification wire shape: method `notifications/message`, params {level, logger?, data} | INFO | ✅ compliant | notifications.rs LoggingMessageNotificationParams (spec-aligned single definition per COMPLIANCE.md; duplicate in logging.rs removed); emission via SessionContext::notify_log builds LoggingMessageNotification (session.rs:481-493); method asserted on the wire in log_gating_2026.rs |
| Servers SHOULD rate limit log messages, include relevant context, use consistent logger names, remove sensitive information | SHOULD | ➖ n/a | Message content/frequency is authored by application tool code via notify_log(level, data, logger, meta) (session.rs:431-437); the framework provides the carrier, not the content policy. No rate limiter exists — deployment concern |
| Log messages MUST NOT contain credentials/secrets, PII, or internal system details aiding attacks | MUST NOT | ➖ n/a | Payloads are application-supplied (session.rs:431-437); the framework cannot enforce content rules. Framework-originated log notifications carry no credentials |
| Clients MAY present log messages in UI, filter/search, display severity, persist | MAY | ➖ n/a | turul-mcp-client surfaces all server notifications through its transport event stream for the embedding app to render; UI presentation is out of library scope |

## Pagination (draft 2026-07-28)

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/utilities/pagination>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cursor is an opaque string token; page size is server-determined and clients MUST NOT assume a fixed page size | MUST NOT | ✅ compliant | Server picks page size (DEFAULT 50, clamp 1..=1000 — handlers/mod.rs:268-279); client passes cursors verbatim with no size assumption (crates/turul-mcp-client/src/client.rs:843-882, cursor.as_str() only); wire echo test crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/wire_compliance.rs:337-363 |
| Server response includes the current page plus optional `nextCursor` when more results exist | INFO | 🟡 partial | All list handlers compute has_more and set nextCursor on the result (prompts handlers/mod.rs:330-354; tools server.rs:1648-1673; resources/templates analogous; 2026 result types carry next_cursor → "nextCursor": tools.rs:309-311, resources.rs:223-225). Behavioral pagination suites exist only on the 2025-11-25-pinned root tests crate (tests/Cargo.toml:10 pins protocol-2025-11-25); no 2026-surface test asserts nextCursor (grep cursor over crates/turul-mcp-server/tests/*_2026.rs: zero) — *Implemented identically for 2026 but untested on the default-2026 wire path.* |
| Client continues pagination by issuing the request with the received `cursor` in params | INFO | ✅ compliant | client.rs list_tools_paginated/list_resources_paginated/list_prompts_paginated (843-882, 1182, 1384); 2026-path test crates/turul-mcp-client/tests/bilingual_2026_operations.rs::paginated_list_routes_through_2026_with_meta_and_cursor (cursor sent alongside required _meta, nextCursor parsed back) |
| Operations supporting pagination: resources/list, resources/templates/list, prompts/list, tools/list | INFO | ✅ compliant | PromptsListHandler (handlers/mod.rs:260-369), ResourcesListHandler and ResourceTemplatesHandler (handlers/mod.rs:1246-1382 for templates), ListToolsHandler (server.rs:1600-1723) all implement cursor+nextCursor with sorted stable ordering |
| Servers SHOULD provide stable cursors | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Cursors are the last item's name over a name-sorted list (handlers/mod.rs:307-339, server.rs:1652-1657) — stable across identical list states; ordering covered by 2025-pinned suites (tests/mcp_compliance_tests.rs et al.) |
| Servers SHOULD handle invalid cursors gracefully | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | Unknown cursor string degrades to position-after (handlers/mod.rs:311-322 unwrap_or(len) → empty page, HTTP 200) — graceful, never panics; but indistinguishable from end-of-results for the client | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:1649-1654 validates cursor against active tool names; invalid cursor returns McpError::InvalidParameters (graceful, never panics). Behavior changed from undistinguished end-of-results to explicit -32602 error per row 716 fix.
| Invalid cursors SHOULD result in an error with code -32602 (Invalid params) | SHOULD | ✅ compliant | No cursor validation exists on any list endpoint — a garbage cursor returns 200 with an empty page (handlers/mod.rs:311-322), never -32602. extract_cursor_from_params accepts any string | — **RE-GRADED 2026-06-12** (post-P2-batch verification): crates/turul-mcp-server/src/server.rs:1649-1654 enforces cursor validation: if !tools.iter().any(/t/ t.name == cursor_name) → McpError::InvalidParameters (code -32602). Test tools_list_is_deterministic_paginated_and_cacheable (wire_edges_2026.rs:348-355) sends 'garbage-cursor' and asserts status 400, code -32602; test passes.
| Clients SHOULD treat a missing `nextCursor` as the end of results and support both paginated and non-paginated flows | SHOULD | 🟡 partial | Paginated APIs return ListXxxResult with Option next_cursor for the caller to terminate on None. BUT the convenience list_tools()/fetch_tools (client.rs:753-840), list_resources (1126), list_prompts (1331) fetch exactly one page and discard nextCursor — silent truncation against a paginating server |
| Clients MUST treat cursors as opaque: no parsing/modification, no determination from the value other than non-null presence (empty string is a valid cursor, MUST NOT be treated as end of results) | MUST | ✅ compliant | Client never inspects cursor content — only as_str() pass-through (client.rs:851-868); no is_empty()/parse on Cursor anywhere in client.rs (grep verified). Empty-string edge has no dedicated test, but no code path exists that could treat it as terminal |

## Schema Reference — pin parity + every normative requirement embedded in the schema page

Spec: <https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/schema>

| Requirement | Level | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| PIN PARITY: live schema/draft/schema.ts identical to vendored pin | INFO | ✅ compliant | Live fetch at immutable upstream commit 93671a3f (and HEAD 62811256), 2026-07-13: sha256 6e4cba2d17f7156877357762b6b4b63cd790d8973f61ec35ab73cd61ad67017d == vendored pin sha256 (byte-identical, full diff = 0 lines, 3151 lines); matches ADR-027 rev-log 2026-06-10 entry and schema/README.md provenance — *NO re-pin trigger exists. Every type/method on the live page is in the pin and vice versa by construction (identical bytes).* |
| LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION = "2026-07-28" — crate wire string must track the schema constant | INFO | ✅ compliant | crates/turul-mcp-protocol-2026-07-28/src/lib.rs:209 (MCP_VERSION), src/version.rs:38 (serde rename + DRAFT-2026-v1 alias); drift-detector test tests/compliance.rs:2061-2066 parses the vendored file and asserts it matches MCP_VERSION |
| PIN PARITY (examples fixtures): upstream schema/draft/examples pin current | INFO | ✅ compliant | schema/EXAMPLES_PIN.md pin 1304c8fe5f07…; GitHub compare 1304c8fe...main returns ahead_by: 0 (2026-06-11) — pin IS upstream HEAD; harness src/compliance/ round-trips 20/20 fixtures — *EXAMPLES_PIN.md still says 'Captured: 2026-05-24' though the SHA was advanced 2026-06-10 — doc-regen nit, see gap G3.* |
